NCK MCQ Compilation — Answers & Rationales (2026)
Compiled from NCK past papers / revision sets. Each question shows all choices with the correct answer in red, followed by a rationale referenced to approved Kenyan nursing materials (NCK curriculum, Kenya MOH/KEPH, IMNCI, Basic Paediatric Protocols, Brunner & Suddarth, Myles Midwifery). MCQs only.
BSCN
BSCN — Paper I
Year: 2022
1. The immediate action in a patient with sickle cell anemia who develops priapism is:-
A. Caution the patient against passing urine till the priapism resolves
B. Make the patient to lie still on bed
C. Have the patient take a warm bath
D. Administer morphine at the onset of the attack
Answer: C. Have the patient take a warm bath
Rationale: In sickle cell disease, priapism is a vaso-occlusive crisis; conservative first-line measures include hydration, warmth (warm bath), emptying the bladder by voiding, and analgesia. A warm bath promotes vasodilation and venous drainage to relieve the engorgement, making it the appropriate immediate nursing action per Brunner & Suddarth and Kenya haematology management.
2. The oral-dental condition is a common complaint among the elderly is:-
A. Halitosis
B. Bruxism
C. Dental abscess
D. Xerostomia
Answer: D. Xerostomia
Rationale: Xerostomia (dry mouth) is the most common oral-dental complaint among the elderly, resulting from reduced salivary flow, polypharmacy and age-related changes. It predisposes to caries, infection and difficulty swallowing, per Kozier Fundamentals and gerontological nursing references.
3. The most appropriate time to administer an antacid is:-
A. With the meal.
B. 30 minutes before the meal.
C. Immediately after the meal.
D. 1 – 3 hours after the meal.
Answer: D. 1 – 3 hours after the meal.
Rationale: Antacids are most effective when acid production and gastric emptying peak; giving them 1-3 hours after meals prolongs their neutralizing action as food buffering wanes. This timing is per pharmacology/therapeutic guidelines for peptic acid disorders.
4. A physician has prescribed Novolog insulin for a client with diabetes mellitus. Which statement indicates that the client knows when the peak action of the insulin occurs:-
A. “I will make sure I eat breakfast within 10 minutes of taking my insulin.”
B. “I will need to carry candy or some form of sugar with me all the time.”
C. “I will eat a snack around three o’clock each afternoon.”
D. “I can save my dessert from supper for a bedtime snack.”
Answer: A. “I will make sure I eat breakfast within 10 minutes of taking my insulin.”
Rationale: Novolog (insulin aspart) is a rapid-acting insulin with onset 10-20 minutes and peak at 1-3 hours; the client must eat within 5-15 minutes to prevent hypoglycaemia at peak action. Eating immediately after dosing demonstrates correct understanding of its rapid onset/peak, per pharmacology guidelines.
5. The nurse prepares a liquid medication and then finds that the client no longer needs the medication. The most appropriate action is:-
A. To keep the count correct, record that the drug was taken
B. Charge for the dose because it must be paid for
C. Record the medication as “not taken” and discard the poured dose
D. Pour the medication back into the container
Answer: C. Record the medication as “not taken” and discard the poured dose
Rationale: A poured/prepared dose that is no longer needed must be discarded (a poured medication is never returned to the stock container due to contamination risk) and documented as not given. This adheres to medication administration safety standards in Kozier Fundamentals.
6. A nurse using Maslows hierarchy of needs would indicate one of the following as a priority Nursing diagnosis:-
A. Ineffective coping
B. Risk for infection
C. Altered comfort
D. Ineffective breathing pattern
Answer: D. Ineffective breathing pattern
Rationale: By Maslow's hierarchy, physiological needs take priority, and oxygenation/breathing is the most basic survival need. Ineffective breathing pattern therefore ranks above infection risk, comfort and coping as the priority nursing diagnosis, per nursing process fundamentals.
7. A client with acute leukemia is admitted to the oncology unit. Which of the following would be most important for the nurse to inquire:-
A. "Have you lost weight recently?"
B. "Have you noticed changes in your alertness?"
C. "Have you noticed a change in sleeping habits recently?"
D. "Have you had a respiratory infection in the last 6 months?"
Answer: D. "Have you had a respiratory infection in the last 6 months?"
Rationale: In acute leukaemia, abnormal proliferation of immature leukocytes causes neutropenia and profound immunosuppression, making infection the leading cause of morbidity and mortality. Assessing for recent/recurrent respiratory infections is most important to guide protective measures, per Brunner & Suddarth oncology nursing.
8. The nurse is admitting a client with Guillain-Barre syndrome to the nursing unit. The client has ascending paralysis to the level of the waist. Knowing the complications of the disorder, the nurse should bring which most essential items into the client’s room:-
A. Nebulizer and pulse oximeter
B. Blood pressure cuff and flashlight
C. Flashlight and incentive spirometer
D. Electrocardiographic monitoring electrodes and intubation tray
Answer: A. Nebulizer and pulse oximeter
Rationale: Ascending paralysis in Guillain-Barre syndrome threatens the respiratory (diaphragmatic) muscles, so respiratory monitoring and support are the most essential preparations. A pulse oximeter continuously monitors oxygenation while a nebulizer supports airway management; per Brunner & Suddarth neurological nursing, respiratory function is the priority in GBS. This is the standard keyed answer.
9. Based on the mechanism of action, the nurse would be right to expect that in this group of antibiotics patient may also have anemia:-
A. Tetracyclins
B. Carbapenems
C. Sulfonamide
D. Penicillin
Answer: C. Sulfonamide
Rationale: Sulfonamides can cause haemolytic anaemia (especially in G6PD deficiency) and megaloblastic/aplastic anaemia through interference with folate metabolism and oxidative haemolysis. Chloramphenicol is the classic anaemia-causer, but among these options sulfonamides are the recognised cause of drug-induced anaemia (Kenya Pharmacology guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth).
10. Examination of a patient in a supine position reveal distended jugular veins from the base of the neck to the angle of the jaw. This finding indicates:-
A. Decreased venous return
B. Increased central venous pressure
C. Increased pulmonary artery capillary pressure
D. Left-sided heart failure
Answer: B. Increased central venous pressure
Rationale: Jugular venous distension extending to the angle of the jaw in a supine (and even in an elevated) position reflects elevated central venous/right atrial pressure, indicating right-sided heart failure or fluid overload. This is the direct clinical correlate of raised CVP (Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing).
11. The pain in acute suppurative otitis media is more severe at:-
A. Night
B. Morning
C. Mid-day
D. All the day
Answer: A. Night
Rationale: In acute suppurative otitis media the earache is characteristically worse at night because recumbency increases venous congestion and middle-ear pressure, intensifying the throbbing pain. This is standard ENT clinical teaching (Brunner & Suddarth).
12. A hydrophilic drug:-
A. Has low ability to penetrate through the cell membrane lipids
B. Penetrates through cell membranes via endocytosis
C. Easily penetrates through blood-brain barrier
D. Is highly reabsorbed in the renal tubules
Answer: A. Has low ability to penetrate through the cell membrane lipids
Rationale: Hydrophilic (water-soluble, often ionised) drugs do not dissolve readily in the lipid bilayer, so they penetrate cell membranes poorly and cross the blood-brain barrier poorly. They also are not highly reabsorbed in renal tubules. This is basic pharmacokinetics (Kenya Pharmacology guidelines).
13. An example of a non-modifiable risk factor for cardiac disease is;
A. Use of oral contraceptives
B. Post-menopause
C. Diabetes mellitus
D. High body mass index
Answer: B. Post-menopause
Rationale: Non-modifiable cardiac risk factors are those that cannot be changed (age, sex, family history, menopausal/hormonal status related to age). Post-menopause is age/sex-related and cannot be altered, whereas oral contraceptive use, diabetes and high BMI are modifiable (Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya MOH cardiovascular guidelines).
14. Clinical Manifestation of hyperthyroidism include:-
A. Hypothermia, weight gain, constipation.
B. Slurred speech, tachycardia, easy bruising.
C. Increased appetite, weight gain, reduced peristalsis.
D. Weight loss, palpitations, and menstrual disturbances.
Answer: D. Weight loss, palpitations, and menstrual disturbances.
Rationale: Hyperthyroidism produces a hypermetabolic state: weight loss despite increased appetite, palpitations/tachycardia, heat intolerance and menstrual irregularities (oligomenorrhoea). The other options describe hypothyroid features (weight gain, constipation, hypothermia) (Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing).
15. A patient has just received 30 mg of codeine by mouth for pain. Five minutes later the patient gets an episode of vomiting. What would be your first nursing intervention;
A. Counsel the patient
B. Observe the emesis
C. Call the physician
D. Remedicate the patient
Answer: B. Observe the emesis
Rationale: The first nursing action is to assess/observe the vomitus to determine whether the codeine tablet was expelled before deciding whether re-medication is needed. Assessment precedes intervention in the nursing process, and this guides whether the dose was retained (Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing).
16. Medical management of the client with diverticulitis should include which of the following treatments:-
A. Increased fiber in diet
B. Reduced fluid intake
C. Exercise to increase intra-abdominal pressure
D. Administration of antibiotics
Answer: D. Administration of antibiotics
Rationale: Acute diverticulitis (inflamed/infected diverticula) is managed with antibiotics, bowel rest and a LOW-fibre/clear-liquid diet during the acute phase; high-fibre diet is for prevention, not acute treatment. Reduced fluids and increasing intra-abdominal pressure are contraindicated (Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing).
17. Chronic dermatitis of unknown etiology in patients with a history of allergy is;
A. Actinic dermatitis
B. Atopic dermatitis
C. Stasis dermatitis
D. Serborrheic dermatitis
Answer: B. Atopic dermatitis
Rationale: Atopic dermatitis is a chronic, relapsing inflammatory skin disease of uncertain (multifactorial) aetiology occurring in individuals with a personal or family history of atopy/allergy (asthma, allergic rhinitis). This matches the description precisely (Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya dermatology guidelines).
18. Plaster casts are used for:-
A. Correction of deformities, preventing deformities
B. Immobilize limbs, support internal fixation devices
C. Correction of deformities, support external fixation
D. Preventing deformities, control heamorrhage
Answer: B. Immobilize limbs, support internal fixation devices
Rationale: Plaster casts function to immobilise limbs/joints, maintain alignment and support internal fixation after reduction of fractures. Correction of deformities and controlling haemorrhage are not primary cast functions. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
19. The Na+ K+ ATPase pump: -
A. Has a coupling ratio of 2:1
B. Is found mainly in the intravascular fluid compartment
C. Extrudes potassium ions from the cell and takes two sodium ions into the cell
D. Extrudes three sodium ions from the cell and takes two potassium ions into the cell
Answer: D. Extrudes three sodium ions from the cell and takes two potassium ions into the cell
Rationale: The Na+/K+ ATPase pumps 3 Na+ out of and 2 K+ into the cell per ATP hydrolysed (3:2 coupling ratio), maintaining the resting membrane potential. It is a plasma-membrane bound protein. Reference: Guyton & Hall / Anatomy & Physiology syllabus.
20. The most appropriate action during the administration of first aid to an individual with severe neck injuries include:-
A. Roll the casualty trunk first as you gently support the extremities, neck and head
B. Extend the head so as to open the airway in case the casualty needs resuscitation
C. Realign the neck with the rest of the body and prevent it from falling backwards
D. Assess CAB and provide resuscitation first if needed before immobilization
Answer: D. Assess CAB and provide resuscitation first if needed before immobilization
Rationale: In first aid the primary survey (Circulation-Airway-Breathing / life over limb) takes priority; resuscitation needs are addressed while maintaining manual in-line neck stabilisation, never extending or realigning the neck. Reference: Kenya First Aid / BLS guidelines.
21. A client has signs of increased intracranial pressure (ICP). Which of the following is an early indicator of deterioration in the client’s condition:-
A. Widening pulse pressure
B. Decrease in pulse rate
C. Dilated fixed pupils
D. Decrease in level of consciousness
Answer: D. Decrease in level of consciousness
Rationale: A declining level of consciousness is the earliest and most sensitive sign of rising ICP. Widening pulse pressure, bradycardia (Cushing's triad) and fixed dilated pupils are late, ominous signs. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
22. Immediately after receiving a patient from surgery, the priority nursing assessment will be:-
A. Incision site
B. Respiration
C. Level of pain
D. The tubes attached
Answer: B. Respiration
Rationale: On receiving a post-operative patient, airway/breathing (respiration) is the immediate ABC priority, especially given anaesthetic-related respiratory depression. Incision, pain and tubes are assessed after airway/breathing are secured. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth; peri-operative nursing.
23. The most appropriate intervention for a casualty with an open fracture of the lower limb would be to:-
A. Lay the casualty down and lower both legs
B. Elevate the head and apply pressure on the fracture site
C. Apply direct pressure on the wound and raise the legs
D. Apply pressure around the wound and support limb
Answer: D. Apply pressure around the wound and support limb
Rationale: For an open fracture with bleeding, apply pressure around (not directly on) the protruding bone/wound to control haemorrhage, then immobilise and support the limb. Direct pressure on the fracture or lowering/raising legs is inappropriate. Reference: Kenya First Aid guidelines.
24. A child with Wilms’ tumor presents with:-
A. Gross hematuria
B. Dysuria
C. Nausea and vomiting
D. An abdominal mass
Answer: D. An abdominal mass
Rationale: Wilms' tumour (nephroblastoma) classically presents as a painless, unilateral abdominal/flank mass discovered in a young child, often incidentally. Haematuria may occur but the hallmark presenting feature is the abdominal mass. Reference: Kenya Basic Paediatric Protocols; paediatric oncology.
25. When assessing a patient for acute glaucoma, you would expect to find:-
A. Clear cornea.
B. Constricted pupil.
C. Marked blurring of vision.
D. Watery occular discharge.
Answer: C. Marked blurring of vision.
Rationale: Acute angle-closure glaucoma presents with sudden severe eye pain, marked blurring of vision with halos, a cloudy/steamy (hazy) cornea and a fixed mid-dilated pupil. Of the options, marked blurring of vision is the expected finding; the cornea is hazy (not clear) and the pupil dilated (not constricted). Reference: Brunner & Suddarth ophthalmic nursing.
26. A patient is to receive 1,000 mL of Normal Saline over 8 hours. The Intravenous administration set has a drop factor of 20 drops per mL. The drip rate in drops per minute will be set at:-
A. 25 drops/min
B. 42 drops/min
C. 50 drops/min
D. 100 drops/min
Answer: B. 42 drops/min
Rationale: Drip rate = (volume x drop factor) / time in minutes = (1000 mL x 20) / (8 x 60) = 20000/480 = 41.6 ~ 42 drops/min. This is the standard IV flow calculation per Kozier/Brunner.
27. The most common cause of recto-vaginal fistula is:-
A. Obstetrical
B. Irradiation of the pelvis
C. Carcinoma
D. Endometriosis
Answer: A. Obstetrical
Rationale: The most common cause of rectovaginal fistula is obstetric trauma, particularly obstructed/prolonged labour and third/fourth degree perineal tears, which is highly relevant in the Kenyan setting. This is consistent with Myles Textbook for Midwives and Kenya EmONC guidelines.
28. A nursing intervention that is most appropriate to decrease post-operative edema and pain following an inguinal herniorraphy is:-
A. Applying a truss to the hernia site
B. Allowing the patient to stand to void
C. Supporting incision during coughing
D. Applying a scrotal support with ice bag
Answer: D. Applying a scrotal support with ice bag
Rationale: Following inguinal herniorrhaphy, scrotal support with an ice bag reduces post-operative scrotal/inguinal edema and pain by minimizing swelling and providing support, per Brunner & Suddarth surgical nursing. Supporting the incision during coughing prevents dehiscence but does not specifically decrease edema.
29. Chvostek’s sign is used for diagnosis of:-
A. Hypercalcemia resulting from hyperparathyroidism.
B. Hypercalcemia resulting from hyperpituitarism.
C. Hypocalcemia resulting from hypoparathyroidism.
D. Hypocalcemia resulting from hypothyroidism.
Answer: C. Hypocalcemia resulting from hypoparathyroidism.
Rationale: Chvostek's sign (facial twitch on tapping the facial nerve) indicates neuromuscular irritability due to hypocalcemia, classically from hypoparathyroidism (e.g., after thyroidectomy). This is standard per Brunner & Suddarth.
30. Salivary glands situated one on each side of the face just below the auditory canal are:-
A. Submandibular
B. Parotid
C. Sublingual
D. Tonsils
Answer: B. Parotid
Rationale: The parotid glands are the largest salivary glands, located one on each side of the face just below and in front of the ear/external auditory canal. This is standard anatomy per Kozier/anatomy texts.
31. Calcium gluconate should be readily available for a patient after thyroidectomy because:-
A. The patient may experience tetany due to destruction of parathyroid glands.
B. There is risk for respiratory distress caused by hematoma .
C. Patients may become hypoglycemic due to inability to swallow.
D. To correct hypercalcaemia that may occur due to reduced urinary output.
Answer: A. The patient may experience tetany due to destruction of parathyroid glands.
Rationale: Inadvertent removal or damage of the parathyroid glands during thyroidectomy causes hypocalcemia leading to tetany; calcium gluconate must be readily available to treat this emergency. This is standard post-thyroidectomy nursing care per Brunner & Suddarth.
32. You are doing a digital rectal examination and you want to palpate the prostate gland; you will palpate the prostate on the:-
A. Anterior wall of the rectum
B. Lateral wall of the rectum
C. Posterior wall of the rectum
D. Proximal part of the rectum
Answer: A. Anterior wall of the rectum
Rationale: During digital rectal examination the prostate gland is palpated on the anterior wall of the rectum, as it lies just anterior to the rectum below the bladder. This is standard clinical examination anatomy per Brunner & Suddarth.
33. A 55-year-old man complaints that he sometimes has sudden pain on one side of his face. It lasts up to 2 minutes. The pain is excruciating and feels like an “electric shock”. He states it happens when he shaves and sometimes when he is chewing food. Upon physical exam, you notice the area of the face he points to is the area of distribution of 5th cranial nerve. He is otherwise healthy and does not take any medications. You ask him if he has ever had shingles and says no. The most likely diagnosis is:-
A. Tic dolourex
B. Cluster headache
C. Bells palsy - cranial nerve 7
D. Temporomandibular joint dysfunction
Answer: A. Tic dolourex
Rationale: Trigeminal neuralgia (tic douloureux) is characterised by sudden, severe, electric-shock-like paroxysms of pain along the distribution of the 5th cranial nerve, triggered by activities such as shaving, chewing or touching the face. The classic trigger points and unilateral facial distribution distinguish it from Bell's palsy (motor weakness) and cluster headache. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
34. Causes of sensorineural hearing loss include:-
A. Aging, impacted cerumen.
B. Ototoxic drugs, brain tumours.
C. Chronic exposure to loud noise, otitis media.
D. Mumps, perforation of tympanic membrane.
Answer: B. Ototoxic drugs, brain tumours.
Rationale: Sensorineural hearing loss results from damage to the cochlea or auditory nerve, caused by ototoxic drugs (aminoglycosides, furosemide) and acoustic/brain tumours. Impacted cerumen, otitis media and tympanic perforation cause conductive loss. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth.
35. A patient has a visual acuity of 20/200. This means that a person can see an object:-
A. 20 feet away
B. 100 feet away.
C. Either 20 or 200 feet away
D. 200 feet away.
Answer: A. 20 feet away
Rationale: Visual acuity of 20/200 means the person sees at 20 feet what a person with normal vision sees at 200 feet. The first number (20 feet) is the standard testing distance at which the patient reads the chart. Reference: Kozier/Brunner & Suddarth ophthalmic assessment.
36. The nurse explains to the mother of a 13-year-old admitted to hospital in a sickle cell crisis that the local tissue damage has been caused by the following:-
A. Lack of oxygen in red blood cells
B. Obstruction to circulation
C. Autoimmune reaction complicated by hypoxia
D. Elevated serum bilirubin concentration
Answer: B. Obstruction to circulation
Rationale: In sickle cell crisis, sickled (rigid, crescent-shaped) red cells clump and occlude small vessels, causing vaso-occlusion. This obstruction to circulation produces local ischaemia, tissue hypoxia and infarction (tissue damage). Reference: Kenya Basic Paediatric Protocols; Brunner & Suddarth.
37. The rationale for use of angiotensin converting enzyme inhibitors particularly in treating patients with chronic kidney disease is;-
A. Increase proteinuria and stabilize renal function
B. Diminish proteinuria and stabilize renal function
C. Increase urine output and stabilize renal function
D. Increase loss of albumin in urine
Answer: B. Diminish proteinuria and stabilize renal function
Rationale: ACE inhibitors reduce intraglomerular pressure by dilating the efferent arteriole, thereby diminishing proteinuria and slowing progression of chronic kidney disease (renoprotection). Reference: Kenya MOH clinical guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth.
38. The dumping Syndrome occurs in patients who have undergone:-
A. Jujenostomy
B. Gastrectomy
C. Proctocolectomy
D. Herniotomy
Answer: B. Gastrectomy
Rationale: Dumping syndrome is a well-recognised complication of gastric surgery (gastrectomy/gastric resection), where rapid emptying of hyperosmolar chyme into the jejunum causes vasomotor and GI symptoms. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
39. You are providing first aid to a client with frostbite on the feet. The second priority in the order of interventions would is,
A. Immerse the feet in warm water at about 40 0 C
B. Remove the victim from the cold environment
C. Administer a pain medication such as paracetamol
D. Apply a loose sterile bulky dressing on the feet
Answer: A. Immerse the feet in warm water at about 40 0 C
Rationale: The first priority in frostbite first aid is removing the victim from the cold environment; the second priority is rapid rewarming by immersion in warm water at about 37-40 degrees C. Rewarming precedes analgesia and dressing. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth emergency care.
40. A goal of care for patients suffering from musculoskeletal disorder does not include:-
A. Maintenance of maximum function, pain relief
B. Improvement of function, encouraging elimination
C. Correction of deformity, promotion of comfort
D. Relief of pain, promotion of comfort
Answer: B. Improvement of function, encouraging elimination
Rationale: Goals for musculoskeletal disorders focus on pain relief, comfort, maintenance/improvement of function and correction of deformity. 'Encouraging elimination' is not a musculoskeletal care goal, making this the option NOT included. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth.
41. In appendicitis, the nurse should look for tenderness at McBurney’s point which is located in the:-
A. Right upper quadrant.
B. Right lower quadrant.
C. Left lower quadrant.
D. Left upper quadrant.
Answer: B. Right lower quadrant.
Rationale: McBurney's point lies in the right lower quadrant, one-third of the distance from the anterior superior iliac spine to the umbilicus, and is the site of maximal tenderness in appendicitis. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth; anatomy.
42. The priority nursing diagnosis for a patient experiencing migraine is:-
A. Acute pain related to biologic and chemical factors
B. Anxiety related to change in the health status.
C. Anxiety related to fear of the prognosis
D. Risk of side effects related to medical therapy
Answer: A. Acute pain related to biologic and chemical factors
Rationale: Migraine is defined by severe headache pain; using Maslow/ABC prioritisation, acute pain is the priority nursing diagnosis over anxiety or risk diagnoses. Pain relief is the immediate patient need. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth; nursing process.
43. A male client has an impairment of cranial nerve II. Specific to this impairment, the nurse would plan to do which of the following to ensure clients to ensure client safety:
A. Speak loudly to the client
B. Test the temperature of the shower water
C. Check the temperature of the food on the delivery tray
D. Provide a clear path for ambulation without obstacles
Answer: D. Provide a clear path for ambulation without obstacles
Rationale: Cranial nerve II (optic nerve) governs vision. Impairment causes visual deficit, so the priority safety measure is ensuring a clear, obstacle-free path to prevent falls and injury during ambulation. Speaking loudly (CN VIII) and testing water/food temperature (sensory) are unrelated to vision (Brunner & Suddarth).
44. The nurse is assigned to care for a female client with complete right-sided hemiparesis. The nurse plans care knowing that this condition;
A. The client has complete bilateral paralysis of arms and legs
B. The client has weakness on the right side of the body including the face and tongue
C. The client has lost the ability to move the right arm but is able walk independently
D. The client has lost the ability to move the left arm but is able to walk independently
Answer: B. The client has weakness on the right side of the body including the face and tongue
Rationale: Hemiparesis means weakness (not total paralysis) of one side of the body. Right-sided hemiparesis involves weakness of the right arm, leg, face and tongue, typically from a left-hemisphere stroke. Complete paralysis would be hemiplegia (Brunner & Suddarth).
45. Patient K has a diagnosis of ruptured appendix and is scheduled for an operation. The patient’s pre-operative care of a client will include: -
A. Give the patient solid food
B. Administering clear fluids only
C. An enema to prepare bowel for surgery
D. Administering intravenous antibiotics
Answer: D. Administering intravenous antibiotics
Rationale: A ruptured appendix causes peritonitis; pre-operative care requires IV broad-spectrum antibiotics to control infection, IV fluids, and NPO status. Enemas are contraindicated as they may worsen perforation/peritonitis, and the patient must be kept nil by mouth, not given solids or fluids (Brunner & Suddarth).
46. Respiratory conditions classified as chronic obstructive pulmonary disease include:-
A. Chronic bronchitis, emphysema, peripheral airways disease.
B. Chronic bronchitis, empyema, asthma.
C. Pulmonary embolism, bronchiectasis, empyema.
D. Chronic bronchitis, pleurisy, bronchiectasis
Answer: A. Chronic bronchitis, emphysema, peripheral airways disease.
Rationale: COPD encompasses chronic bronchitis, emphysema and peripheral (small) airways disease, characterised by persistent airflow limitation. Empyema, pulmonary embolism, pleurisy and bronchiectasis are not classified as COPD (Brunner & Suddarth).
47. During the analysis of cerebral spinal fluid in the diagnosis of meningitis .The type of meningitis where there is cerebral spinal fluid glucose is normal and present of lymphocytes is:-
A. Viral meningitis
B. Tuberculosis meningitis
C. Meningococcal meningitis
D. Pneumococcal meningitis
Answer: A. Viral meningitis
Rationale: In viral (aseptic) meningitis, CSF glucose is normal with a lymphocytic (mononuclear) pleocytosis. Bacterial meningitis (meningococcal, pneumococcal) shows low glucose with neutrophils; TB meningitis shows low glucose with lymphocytes (Kenya clinical guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth).
48. Clinical manifestations of pyloric stenosis include:-
A. Failure to thrive, tenesmus
B. Projectile vomiting, colicky abdominal pains
C. Constipation, olive-like mass in upper abdomen
D. Observable peristaltic movements, diarrhea
Answer: B. Projectile vomiting, colicky abdominal pains
Rationale: Pyloric stenosis classically presents with non-bilious projectile vomiting after feeds, hunger/colicky discomfort, visible peristalsis and a palpable olive-like mass. Among the options, projectile vomiting with colicky pain is the correct manifestation set; constipation with olive mass mixes a correct sign with an atypical one (Kenya Basic Paediatric Protocols; Nelson).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
49. The drugs of choice in management of chronic gouty arthritis are:-
A. Colchicine, NSAIDS.
B. Allopurinol, probenecid.
C. Colchicine, sulfinipyrazone.
D. NSAIDS, Allopurinol.
Answer: B. Allopurinol, probenecid.
Rationale: Chronic gouty arthritis management targets serum urate lowering: allopurinol (xanthine oxidase inhibitor) reduces uric acid production and probenecid (uricosuric) enhances excretion. Colchicine and NSAIDs treat acute attacks rather than chronic disease (Kenya therapeutic guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth).
50. A full thickness burn is:-
A. Classified by appearance of blisters.
B. Identified by destruction of entire dermis.
C. Not associated with edema formation.
D. Usually very painful because of exposed nerve endings.
Answer: B. Identified by destruction of entire dermis.
Rationale: A full-thickness (third-degree) burn destroys the entire epidermis and dermis, including nerve endings, so it is typically painless (not painful) and appears dry/leathery rather than blistered. Destruction of the entire dermis is the defining feature (Brunner & Suddarth).
51. Immediate complication of fractures include:-
A. Gangrene, pneumonia
B. Haemorrhage, pneumonia
C. Skin loss, haemorrahage
D. Pneumonia, loss of function
Answer: B. Haemorrhage, pneumonia
Rationale: Immediate complications of fractures include haemorrhage (with shock), and early respiratory complications; among the options haemorrhage with pneumonia best fits early/immediate complications. Gangrene and loss of function are later complications (Brunner & Suddarth).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
52. Furuncles are commonly caused by:-
A. Streptococcus pyogenes.
B. Staphylococcus aureus.
C. Pseudomonas aeroginosa.
D. Clostridium tetani
Answer: B. Staphylococcus aureus.
Rationale: Furuncles (boils) are deep hair-follicle infections most commonly caused by Staphylococcus aureus. Streptococcus pyogenes typically causes cellulitis/erysipelas, Pseudomonas causes folliculitis in other settings, and Clostridium tetani causes tetanus (Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya IPC guidelines).
53. A patient Kante, 16 years old is brought to the Emergency department with history of sodium hypochloride/JIK spill on the left eye 5 minutes ago. The first most appropriate Nurse action would be:-
A. Immediately refer to an ophthalmologist for review
B. Administer topical antibiotics and analgesics
C. Test for Visual Acuity to check extent of damage
D. Patch the eye after irrigation with clean water
Answer: D. Patch the eye after irrigation with clean water
Rationale: For a chemical (alkaline JIK/sodium hypochlorite) eye burn, the immediate priority is copious irrigation of the eye with clean water or saline to dilute and remove the corrosive agent and limit tissue damage, per emergency ophthalmic first-aid guidelines. Of the given options, irrigation is the only one that appears, though patching should not routinely follow. Referral, acuity testing and medication all come after decontamination. Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya emergency care guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
54. Drug route of administration associated with first-pass metabolism is:-
A. Intravenous
B. Intramuscular
C. Sublingual
D. Oral
Answer: D. Oral
Rationale: Orally administered drugs are absorbed from the GI tract into the portal circulation and pass through the liver before reaching systemic circulation, undergoing first-pass (presystemic) metabolism. IV, IM and sublingual routes bypass the portal circulation and avoid first-pass effect. Basic pharmacology (Kenya Pharmacology guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth).
55. Management of Cushing’s syndrome include:-
A. Administration of spironolactone, daily weight taking, fluid restriction
B. Monitoring blood glucose levels, providing a high sodium diet, antibiotic therapy
C. Assessing signs of hyperkalemia, input and output monitoring, encouraging high protein diet
D. Encourage ambulation, plenty of fluids, H2 receptor antagonist therapy
Answer: A. Administration of spironolactone, daily weight taking, fluid restriction
Rationale: Cushing's syndrome causes excess cortisol with sodium/water retention, oedema, hypertension and hypokalaemia; management includes a potassium-sparing agent such as spironolactone, daily weight monitoring and fluid restriction to control fluid overload, plus a low-sodium diet. The other options recommend inappropriate measures (high sodium, high fluids). Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
56. Major risk factors for cerebral vascular accident include:-
A. Trauma, high alcohol intake, positive family history
B. Arterial hypertension, trauma, hyperlipidemia
C. Arterial hypertension, hyperlipidemia, cigarette smoking
D. Positive family history, hyperlipidemia, high alcohol intake
Answer: C. Arterial hypertension, hyperlipidemia, cigarette smoking
Rationale: The major modifiable risk factors for cerebrovascular accident (stroke) are hypertension (the single most important), hyperlipidaemia and cigarette smoking. Trauma and family history are not the principal risk factors, making the combination in option C the most correct. Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya MOH stroke guidelines.
57. The client being seen in a physician’s office has just been scheduled for a barium swallow the next day. The nurse writes down which of the following instructions for the client to follow before the test:-
A. Eat regular supper and breakfast
B. Fast for 8 hours before the test
C. Monitor own bowel movement pattern for constipation
D. Continue to take all oral medications as scheduled
Answer: B. Fast for 8 hours before the test
Rationale: A barium swallow requires the client to be NPO (fasting) for about 8 hours before the procedure so the upper GI tract is empty for clear visualisation. Post-procedure the nurse monitors bowel patterns for constipation from retained barium, but the pre-test instruction is fasting. Brunner & Suddarth diagnostic nursing.
58. A client arrives in the emergency department after a motor vehicle accident. Nursing assessment findings include BP 80/34, pulse rate 120, and respirations 20. Which is the client’s most appropriate priority nursing diagnosis?
A. Alteration in cerebral tissue perfusion
B. Fluid volume deficit
C. Ineffective airway clearance
D. Alteration in sensory perception
Answer: B. Fluid volume deficit
Rationale: The findings (BP 80/34, pulse 120, tachycardia) after trauma indicate hypovolaemic shock from blood loss, so fluid volume deficit is the priority nursing diagnosis needing fluid resuscitation. Airway is patent (respirations 20 and unlaboured) and there is no evidence of airway compromise, so ABC is satisfied and circulation/volume takes priority here. Brunner & Suddarth emergency/shock nursing.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
59. A patient with diabetic ketoacidosis presents with hyperventilation because:-
A. The respiratory centre in the brain is damaged
B. The body PH is low
C. There is high rate of oxygen consumption
D. There is reduced excretion of waste through the renal system
Answer: B. The body PH is low
Rationale: In diabetic ketoacidosis, accumulation of ketoacids lowers blood pH (metabolic acidosis); the body compensates by deep, rapid Kussmaul respirations to blow off CO2 and raise pH. Hence hyperventilation is driven by the low pH. Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya diabetes management guidelines.
60. The best nursing position for a patient following total hip replacement would be.
A. Sims
B. Prone
C. Semi-fowlers
D. Trendelenburg
Answer: C. Semi-fowlers
Rationale: After total hip replacement the hip must not be flexed beyond 90 degrees and the leg kept in abduction with neutral rotation to prevent dislocation. Of the options, semi-Fowler's (limited head elevation) is the safest tolerable position; prone, Sims and Trendelenburg risk hip adduction/malposition or excessive flexion. Brunner & Suddarth orthopaedic nursing.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
61. In regard to Coronary artery disease (CAD). Select the most correct statement: -
A. Does not present with high levels of cardiac enzymes like in myocardial infarction
B. Sudden cardiac death is an event that is due to a decrease in blood supply from CAD
C. Positron emission tomography (PET) is the definitive diagnostic procedure for CAD
D. Long acting nitrates e.g. P.O isosorbide dinitrate 50-100 mg QID can be given
Answer: B. Sudden cardiac death is an event that is due to a decrease in blood supply from CAD
Rationale: Coronary artery disease reduces myocardial blood supply and is the leading cause of sudden cardiac death through fatal ischaemia and arrhythmia. The other statements are false: CAD can raise cardiac enzymes when ischaemia occurs, PET is not the definitive diagnostic test (coronary angiography is), and the stated nitrate dose is excessive/incorrect. Brunner & Suddarth cardiovascular nursing.
62. The appropriate health message to a patient who develops stomatitis secondary to radiotherapy is :-
A. Drink plenty of citrus juices
B. Gurgle with mouthwash and rinse thoroughly after each meal.
C. Use ice cold liquids such as tea or cola to relieve discomfort.
D. Use a toothbrush soaked in saline to clean the mouth.
Answer: D. Use a toothbrush soaked in saline to clean the mouth.
Rationale: For radiotherapy-induced stomatitis, gentle oral care with a soft toothbrush and saline (bland, non-irritating) rinses is recommended; citrus juices, cola and commercial/alcohol-containing mouthwashes are acidic or irritating and worsen the inflamed mucosa. Soft-brush saline care is the appropriate health message. Brunner & Suddarth oncology nursing.
63. The nurse has given suggestions to a client with trigeminal neuralgia about strategies to minimize episodes of pain. The nurse determines that the client needs further teaching if the client makes which statement:-
A. “I will wash my face with cotton pads.”
B. “I’ll have to start chewing on my unaffected side.”
C. “I should not rinse my mouth if toothbrushing is painful.”
D. “I should rinse my mouth if toothbrushing is painful.”
Answer: A. “I will wash my face with cotton pads.”
Rationale: In trigeminal neuralgia any tactile facial stimulation, including washing the face with cotton pads, can trigger paroxysmal pain, so this statement indicates the client needs further teaching (Brunner & Suddarth). Correct self-care includes chewing on the unaffected side and rinsing the mouth when toothbrushing is painful, so options B and D reflect appropriate understanding. The statement showing a misconception is washing the face with cotton pads.
64. The most common outcome of obstructed blood flow to the liver is:-
A. Oesphageal varises
B. Biliary cirrhosis
C. Portal hypertension
D. Liver cirrhosis
Answer: C. Portal hypertension
Rationale: Obstruction of blood flow through the liver raises pressure in the portal venous system, producing portal hypertension, which is the direct and most common consequence. Oesophageal varices and ascites are downstream complications of the portal hypertension itself. Per Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
65. An example of a local factor that affects wound healing is:-
A. Desiccation
B. Circulation
C. Nutrition status
D. Drug therapy
Answer: A. Desiccation
Rationale: Desiccation (drying of the wound surface) is a local factor acting directly at the wound site that impairs epithelial migration and healing. Circulation, nutritional status and drug therapy are systemic factors. Per Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing and Brunner & Suddarth.
66. An patient’s X-ray shows accumulation of thick, purulent fluid within the pleural space with fibrin development. The most likely condition with the patient is;
A. Stasis
B. Pleurisy
C. Empyema
D. Atelectasis
Answer: C. Empyema
Rationale: Accumulation of thick, purulent fluid in the pleural space with fibrin deposition defines empyema. Pleurisy is inflammation of the pleura without pus, and atelectasis is lung collapse. Per Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
67. 65-year-old man undergoes total knee revision without complication. Routine intraoperative cultures are submitted that are positive for growth of coagulase negative staphylococcus at 48 hours postoperative in 3 of 5 specimens. The patient is afebrile and his wound is dry. Appropriate treatment should include:-
A. No further antibiotic therapies
B. Irrigation and debridement with retention of the components
C. Six weeks of parenteral antibiotics
D. Irrigation and debridement with removal of components
Answer: C. Six weeks of parenteral antibiotics
Rationale: Growth of coagulase-negative staphylococcus in 3 of 5 intraoperative cultures meets the threshold for a genuine (not contaminant) periprosthetic infection, indicating deep infection requiring treatment. With well-fixed components and no acute wound breakdown, standard management is component retention with a course of parenteral antibiotics (approximately six weeks). Per orthopaedic/surgical infection guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
68. Myopia is commonly caused by:-
A. Too short eye ball
B. Decreased curvature of the lens
C. A backward displacement of the lens on the globe
D. Increased retroactive ludices of the cornea, lens and aqueous
Answer: D. Increased retroactive ludices of the cornea, lens and aqueous
Rationale: Myopia (near-sightedness) results from light focusing in front of the retina, caused by an excessively long eyeball or increased refractive power (refractive indices/curvature) of the cornea and lens. Among the options, increased refractive index of the ocular media produces this excess convergence. A too-short eyeball causes hyperopia, not myopia. Per Brunner & Suddarth.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
69. A client has autoimmune thrombocytopenic purpura. To determine the client's response to treatment, the nurse would monitor:-
A. Platelet count
B. White blood cell count
C. Potassium levels
D. Partial prothrombin time
Answer: A. Platelet count
Rationale: Autoimmune (idiopathic) thrombocytopenic purpura is characterised by immune destruction of platelets; response to treatment (steroids, IVIG, splenectomy) is evaluated by a rising platelet count. WBC, potassium and PTT do not reflect the disorder. Per Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing.
70. The nursing management of the patient with cholecystitis associated with cholelithiasis is based on the knowledge that:-
A. A low-fat diet is recommended
B. Gallstones once removed tend not to recur
C. Meperidine is to be avoided in the management of pain
D. The disorder can be successfully treated with oral bile salts that dissolve gallstones
Answer: A. A low-fat diet is recommended
Rationale: In cholecystitis with cholelithiasis, a low-fat diet reduces gallbladder stimulation and pain. Gallstones can recur, meperidine is no longer specifically preferred but is not absolutely avoided, and oral bile salt dissolution is only rarely and partially effective. The reliable nursing knowledge is the low-fat dietary recommendation. Per Brunner & Suddarth.
71. A client is discharged home with a prescription for Coumadin (sodium warfarin). The client should be instructed to:-
A. Have a Protime done monthly
B. Eat more fruits and vegetables
C. Drink more liquids
D. Avoid crowds
Answer: A. Have a Protime done monthly
Rationale: Warfarin (Coumadin) requires regular monitoring of prothrombin time (PT/INR) to keep dosing within therapeutic range and prevent bleeding or clotting. Patients should also maintain consistent (not increased) vitamin K intake, so eating more green vegetables would interfere with control. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing; Kenya therapeutic guidelines.
72. A type of wound debridement where topical chemicals are used to break down and liquefy  wound debris is:-
A. Autolytic
B. Surgical
C. Enzymatic
D. Mechanical
Answer: C. Enzymatic
Rationale: Enzymatic debridement uses topical chemical/proteolytic enzymes applied to the wound to break down and liquefy necrotic debris. Autolytic uses the body's own enzymes under moist dressings, surgical uses instruments, and mechanical uses physical force such as wet-to-dry dressings. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth wound care.
73. A nurse anticipates that the type of traction that will be used for a 16 Kgs client with a fractured femur is:-
A. Bryant’s traction
B. Dunlop’s traction
C. Weil sling
D. Buck’s extension traction
Answer: D. Buck’s extension traction
Rationale: For a 16 kg (older child/adolescent) with a fractured femur, Buck's extension (skin traction) is appropriate. Bryant's traction is reserved for young children under about 2 years or weighing under ~12–14 kg where both legs are suspended vertically. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals; orthopaedic nursing.
74. Primary role of hydrochloric acid in the digestive process is to:-
A. Break down carbohydrates into simple sugars
B. Activate pepsinogen to pepsin
C. Prevent mechanical injury to the stomach wall
D. Emulsification of fats and fatty acids
Answer: B. Activate pepsinogen to pepsin
Rationale: Hydrochloric acid secreted by gastric parietal cells provides the acidic environment that converts inactive pepsinogen into active pepsin for protein digestion, and also kills ingested pathogens. It does not digest carbohydrates or emulsify fats (bile does that). Reference: Anatomy & Physiology (Tortora/Guyton).
75. Pemphigus is a skin disorder characterized by:-
A. Papules of different shapes
B. Macules that are fluid filled
C. Vesicles of various sizes
D. Nodules with pus
Answer: C. Vesicles of various sizes
Rationale: Pemphigus is an autoimmune blistering disorder characterized by flaccid vesicles and bullae of varying sizes that rupture easily, with a positive Nikolsky sign. The lesions are fluid-filled blisters rather than papules, macules or pustular nodules. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth dermatology.
76. A nurse is showing a patient how to use crutches at home and instructing his mother about how to change his bandages. At this time, the nurse is primarily acting as:-
A. Patient advocate
B. Manager of care
C. Teacher
D. Decision maker
Answer: C. Teacher
Rationale: Demonstrating crutch use and instructing the mother on changing bandages are health education activities, so the nurse is functioning primarily in the teacher/educator role. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing (nursing roles).
77. Clinical manifestation of bronchial asthma are:-
A. Wheezing, dyspnea, vomiting
B. Restlessness, wheezing, headache
C. Wheezing, dyspnea, coughing
D. Apprehension, restlessness, vomiting
Answer: C. Wheezing, dyspnea, coughing
Rationale: The classic triad of bronchial asthma is wheezing, dyspnoea and cough (often with chest tightness) due to reversible airway obstruction. Vomiting and headache are not characteristic manifestations. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya clinical guidelines.
78. A key instruction to give a patient when collecting a clean-catch midstream urine sample is:-
A. Collect the first few drops of urine
B. Avoid drinking fluids for 8 hours before collection
C. Clean the genital area before collecting the sample
D. Start urinating directly into the collection container
Answer: C. Clean the genital area before collecting the sample
Rationale: A clean-catch midstream specimen requires cleansing the genital/meatal area first to reduce contamination, then discarding the first stream and collecting midstream urine into a sterile container. The first drops are discarded, and fluid restriction is unnecessary. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals; Kenya IPC/lab guidelines.
79. An adult patient weighing 120 kg has a 20% total body surface area burn. Using the modified parkland formula, how much total intravenous fluid should he receive in the first 8 hours:-
A. 2400 mL
B. 4800mL
C. 9600 mL
D. 10,800 ml
Answer: B. 4800mL
Rationale: Parkland formula = 4 mL x weight (kg) x %TBSA = 4 x 120 x 20 = 9600 mL total in 24 hours, with half given in the first 8 hours = 4800 mL. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth burns management; Kenya emergency guidelines.
80. Complications associated with Tracheo-oesophageal atresia include:-
A. Pneumonia, malnutrition, intestinal obstruction
B. Stenosis of the oesophagus, leakage into the mediastinum, intestinal obstruction.
C. Abdominal distension, constipation,fitula formation
D. Gastro-esophageal reflux, esophagus constriction, leakage into chest cavity.
Answer: B. Stenosis of the oesophagus, leakage into the mediastinum, intestinal obstruction.
Rationale: Tracheo-oesophageal atresia/fistula predisposes to aspiration pneumonia and, following surgical repair, anastomotic stricture (oesophageal stenosis) and anastomotic leak into the mediastinum. Option B best captures oesophageal stenosis and mediastinal leakage as recognised complications. Reference: Kenya Basic Paediatric Protocols; paediatric surgery texts.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
81. Cerebral hemorrhage located under the dura matter is:-
A. Epidural hematoma.
B. Extradural hematoma.
C. Intracerebral hematoma.
D. Subdural hematoma
Answer: D. Subdural hematoma
Rationale: A subdural hematoma is a collection of blood located beneath (under) the dura mater, typically from torn bridging veins. Epidural/extradural hematomas lie above the dura, and intracerebral hematomas are within brain tissue (Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing).
82. For proper diagnosis and management of respiratory conditions, various diagnostic procedures can be performed. A procedure that utilizes a spirometer is: -
A. Arterial blood gases
B. Pulmonary function tests
C. Pulse oximetry
D. Serial blood gas analysis
Answer: B. Pulmonary function tests
Rationale: Pulmonary function tests (spirometry) use a spirometer to measure lung volumes and airflow. Arterial blood gases, pulse oximetry and serial blood gas analysis do not use a spirometer (Brunner & Suddarth Medical-Surgical Nursing).
83. Reabsorption in the renal tubule occurs:-
A. Passively to transport sugar and amino acids
B. Actively to promote water reabsorption
C. By the antidiuretic hormone decreasing permeability at the tubules
D. Due to high aldosterone that reduces sodium excretion
Answer: A. Passively to transport sugar and amino acids
Rationale: Tubular reabsorption occurs by both active and passive mechanisms; glucose and amino acids are reabsorbed via carrier-mediated transport. Among these options the best fit is that reabsorption occurs to transport sugars and amino acids. ADH increases (not decreases) permeability to water, and aldosterone increases sodium reabsorption, making the other options incorrect (Guyton/Kozier physiology).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
84. Imbalance nutrition related to stress hormone in diabetes mellitus is treated by:-
A. Relevant medication, Food, Insulin and Physical activity
B. Physical activity, Relevant medication, Health Education and life style modification
C. Insulin, Life style medication, Health Education, Food
D. Food, Health Education, Life modification, Physical activity
Answer: B. Physical activity, Relevant medication, Health Education and life style modification
Rationale: Management of altered nutrition in diabetes mellitus involves the comprehensive package of physical activity, appropriate medication, health education and lifestyle modification. This best reflects holistic diabetes management per Kenya MOH diabetes guidelines and Brunner & Suddarth.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
85. Appropriate drug for treatment of anaphylactic shock is:-
A. Norepinephrine
B. Phenylephrine
C. Dobutamine
D. Epinephrine
Answer: D. Epinephrine
Rationale: Epinephrine (adrenaline) is the first-line drug for anaphylactic shock, given IM 0.5 mg (1:1000). It reverses bronchospasm, vasodilation and hypotension. The other vasopressors are not the treatment of choice for anaphylaxis (Kenya MOH emergency guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth).
86. Gram negative bacilli include:-
A. Escherichia coli, Salmonella, Shigella
B. Salmonella, Staphylococcus, Streptococcus.
C. Shigella, Neisseria gonorrhea, Streptococci
D. Clostridium tetani, Escherichia coli, Staphylococcus.
Answer: A. Escherichia coli, Salmonella, Shigella
Rationale: Escherichia coli, Salmonella and Shigella are all Gram-negative bacilli. The other options include Gram-positive organisms (Staphylococcus, Streptococcus, Clostridium) or Gram-negative diplococci (Neisseria), so are incorrect (Kenya microbiology/NCK-endorsed texts).
87. The fundal height immediately after the expulsion of the placenta is felt at:-
A. The level of the umbilicus.
B. 7.5 cm above the symphysis pubis.
C. 4 cm below the umbilicus.
D. At the level of the symphysis pubis.
Answer: A. The level of the umbilicus.
Rationale: Immediately after expulsion of the placenta, the well-contracted uterine fundus is felt at the level of the umbilicus. Thereafter it involutes by about 1 cm per day (Myles Textbook for Midwives; Kenya EmONC guidelines).
88. The midwife checks for the presence of the cord around the neck during the second stage of labour on:-
A. Extension of the head
B. Crowing of the head
C. Restitution
D. Birth of the head
Answer: D. Birth of the head
Rationale: After the birth (delivery) of the head, before restitution and delivery of the shoulders, the midwife feels around the neck to check for a nuchal cord (cord around the neck). This is the recognised point for checking in the second stage of labour (Myles Textbook for Midwives; Kenya EmONC).
89. A ring of tight muscle that forms around the body or neck of fetus is:-
A. Bundls’ ring
B. Bundle of his
C. Constriction ring
D. Retraction ring
Answer: C. Constriction ring
Rationale: A constriction ring is a localised, persistent contraction/spasm of a ring of uterine muscle that forms around the fetal neck or body (often at a junction), typically due to uncoordinated uterine action. Bandl's ring is a retraction ring seen in obstructed labour, distinct from a constriction ring. Per Myles Textbook for Midwives.
90. When the chorion detaches from lower uterine segment due to increased intrauterine pressure and forms a sac of fluid that bulge downward into the internal os is called: -
A. Rupture of membranes
B. Formation of upper and lower uterine segments
C. Formation of fore waters
D. Intensity and resting tone
Answer: C. Formation of fore waters
Rationale: As the chorion detaches from the lower uterine segment and increased intrauterine pressure pushes fluid ahead of the presenting part into the internal os, the bulging bag of fluid is the forewaters. This is a normal mechanism of the first stage. Per Myles Textbook for Midwives.
91. Mechanical factors that facilitates progress of labour during first stage include:-
A. General fluid pressure, foetal axis pressure
B. Cervical dilatation, fundal dominance
C. Polarity, cervical effacement
D. Fundal dominance, fetal axis pressure
Answer: A. General fluid pressure, foetal axis pressure
Rationale: Mechanical factors aiding first-stage progress are general fluid pressure (even distribution of intrauterine pressure via the forewaters) and fetal axis pressure (force transmitted down the fetal long axis). Fundal dominance and polarity describe the physiology of contractions rather than mechanical factors, and cervical dilatation/effacement are effects. Per Myles Textbook for Midwives.
92. If delivery occurs unexpectedly while the patient is on Heparin and excessive bleeding occurs, what is the appropriate initial intervention:-
A. Administaration of tranexemic acid.
B. Ligation of the hypogastric arteries.
C. Infusion of fresh frozen plasma.
D. Administration of protamine acetate.
Answer: D. Administration of protamine acetate.
Rationale: Heparin-induced bleeding is reversed with protamine, which binds and neutralises heparin. Among the options this is the specific antidote for heparin, so it is the appropriate initial intervention. (Standard preparation is protamine sulphate; the option's wording is the intended answer.) Per Kenya/standard pharmacology guidelines.
93. A 33 year female at 37 weeks gestation confirmed by early sonography presents with moderate severe vaginal bleeding, she is noted to have placenta previa, which of the following is the best management for her :-
A. Caesarian Section
B. Expectant management
C. Artificial rupture of membrane
D. Give tocolytic drugs
Answer: A. Caesarian Section
Rationale: At 37 weeks (term) with confirmed placenta praevia and moderate-to-severe vaginal bleeding, the definitive and safest management is delivery by caesarean section. Expectant/tocolytic management is only for preterm gestation with minimal bleeding, and ARM is contraindicated in praevia. Per Kenya EmONC/obstetric guidelines and Myles.
94. The active management of third stage of labor in sequence include:-
A. Control cord traction, massage uterus and administer uterotonic
B. Administer uterotonic, massage uterus and control cord traction
C. Control cord traction, administer uterotonic and massage uterus
D. Administer uterotonic, controlled cord traction and massage uterus.
Answer: D. Administer uterotonic, controlled cord traction and massage uterus.
Rationale: Active management of the third stage in correct sequence is: give a uterotonic (oxytocin within one minute of birth), controlled cord traction to deliver the placenta, then uterine massage after placental delivery. Per Kenya EmONC/AMTSL guidelines and WHO.
95. A nurse observation to a patient on heparin is:-
A. Gentle movement in bed when pain subsides
B. Monitor colour and amount of lochia
C. Ensure protamine sulphate is available
D. Continue breastfeeding
Answer: C. Ensure protamine sulphate is available
Rationale: A key nursing responsibility for a patient on heparin is to keep the antidote, protamine sulphate, readily available in case of haemorrhage from over-anticoagulation. This is the safety-focused observation/precaution specific to heparin therapy. Per standard pharmacology/nursing references.
96. The maneuver used in the delivery of extended arms in shoulder dystocia is:-
A. Rubin’s maneuver
B. Roberts maneuver
C. Burnmashall maneuver
D. Loverset maneuver
Answer: D. Loverset maneuver
Rationale: Lovset's manoeuvre is used to deliver extended (nuchal) arms, classically in breech delivery; the same rotational principle is applied to free extended arms. Rubin's and Woods (screw) are internal rotation manoeuvres and McRoberts is positional for shoulder dystocia. Per Myles Textbook for Midwives.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
97. The part of foetal presentation used to describe the position is referred to as:-
A. Attitude
B. Lie
C. Fetal axis
D. Denominator
Answer: D. Denominator
Rationale: The denominator is the named part of the presentation used to describe fetal position (e.g. occiput for vertex, sacrum for breech, mentum for face). Attitude is the relation of fetal parts to each other, and lie is the relation of the fetal to maternal long axis. Per Myles Textbook for Midwives.
98. The correct regimen for administering magnesium Sulphate loading dose intramuscularly is:-
A. 5 g given as a divided dose in each of the buttocks over a period of 10-15 minutes
B. 10 g as a divided dose in each of the buttock
C. 4g as divided dose in each buttock over a period of not less than 5 minutes
D. 10 g as a divided dose in each of the buttock over a period 4 hourly
Answer: A. 5 g given as a divided dose in each of the buttocks over a period of 10-15 minutes
Rationale: The Pritchard IM loading regimen for magnesium sulphate gives 10 g total IM as 5 g deep into each buttock (with 4 g IV loading). Among the options, 5 g in each buttock correctly reflects the divided IM loading dose. Per Kenya EmONC/pre-eclampsia-eclampsia guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
99. In management of uterine inversion:-
A. Oxytocin is administered before the correction of the inversion to control bleeding.
B. Antibiotics are administered during the correction of the uterine inversion.
C. Oxytocin is administered after the inversion of the uterus is corrected.
D. Intravenous fluids are not necessary during the management of uterine inversion.
Answer: C. Oxytocin is administered after the inversion of the uterus is corrected.
Rationale: In uterine inversion, oxytocics are WITHHELD until the uterus is repositioned, because giving them before correction contracts the uterus around the inversion and prevents replacement. Once the fundus is restored, oxytocin is given to maintain contraction and control haemorrhage. IV fluids/resuscitation are essential (Myles Textbook for Midwives; Kenya EmONC).
100. Factors that determine safety of the second stage of labour, that must be carefully monitored include:-
A. Fetal and maternal conditions, descent of the presenting part, asepsis.
B. Uterine contractions, fetal and maternal conditions, descent of the presenting part.
C. Uterine contractions, state of the membranes, descent of the presenting part.
D. Fetal and maternal conditions, asepsis, state of membranes.
Answer: B. Uterine contractions, fetal and maternal conditions, descent of the presenting part.
Rationale: Safe conduct of the second stage requires monitoring uterine contractions (strength/frequency), fetal condition (FHR) and maternal condition (vitals, effort), and progressive descent of the presenting part. These are the core safety parameters; asepsis is important but the best-fitting option combining the key dynamic monitoring parameters is B (Myles; Kenya EmONC partograph principles).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
101. A nurse intervention after membranes ruptures with cord pulsating and prolapsed out of vagina is:-
A. Leave the cord hanging from origin
B. Position on knee chest position
C. Cover cord with a green towel
D. Avoid tocolytics if available
Answer: B. Position on knee chest position
Rationale: Cord prolapse is an emergency; the mother is placed in knee-chest (or exaggerated Sims/Trendelenburg) position to relieve pressure of the presenting part on the cord. The cord is not handled or left exposed to cold, and tocolytics may be USED to reduce contractions, so 'avoid tocolytics' is wrong. Knee-chest positioning is the correct nursing intervention (Kenya EmONC; Myles).
102. A Rh negative women has increase the chances of being immunized during pregnancy when:-
A. Performing External cephalic version.
B. Maternal anemia.
C. Premature labor.
D. Maternal Thyrotoxicosis.
Answer: A. Performing External cephalic version.
Rationale: Rh isoimmunisation occurs when fetomaternal haemorrhage exposes an Rh-negative mother to Rh-positive fetal cells. External cephalic version manipulates the uterus and can cause fetomaternal transfusion, increasing sensitisation risk (an indication for anti-D). Anaemia, preterm labour and thyrotoxicosis do not directly cause isoimmunisation (Myles; Kenya ANC guidelines).
103. During the 2nd trimester of pregnancy, the blood pressure falls due to:-
A. Effects of progesterone on the smooth muscles causing relaxation and dilatation of arterial wall
B. Effects of estrogen on the smooth muscles causing relaxation and dilatation of arterial wall
C. Effects of progesterone on the smooth muscles causing relaxation
D. Effects on estrogen on smooth muscles causing relaxation
Answer: A. Effects of progesterone on the smooth muscles causing relaxation and dilatation of arterial wall
Rationale: In the second trimester BP falls because progesterone relaxes vascular smooth muscle, dilating the arterial walls and lowering peripheral resistance. The most complete correct description is progesterone-mediated relaxation AND dilatation of the arterial wall (Myles; maternal physiology).
104. Foetal hypoxia in first stage of labour is promptly identified by:-
A. Observing foetal heart rate quarter (1/4) hourly, check for moulding
B. Observing foetal heart rate half (1/2 ) hourly, check for meconium
C. Observing foetal heart rate half (1/2) hourly, check for moulding
D. Observing foetal heart rate quarter (1/4) hourly, check for meconium
Answer: D. Observing foetal heart rate quarter (1/4) hourly, check for meconium
Rationale: Fetal hypoxia is detected by monitoring the FHR every 30 minutes in early first stage and every 15 minutes (quarter-hourly) in active/late first stage, together with observing for meconium-stained liquor (a sign of hypoxia). Moulding indicates obstruction/CPD, not directly hypoxia; the hypoxia indicators are frequent FHR and meconium (Kenya partograph/EmONC; Myles).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
105. A major side effect of oxytocin is:-
A. Sodium and water retention
B. Peripheral neuropathy
C. Hypertension
D. Nephrotoxicity
Answer: A. Sodium and water retention
Rationale: Oxytocin has structural similarity to ADH (vasopressin) and has an antidiuretic effect, causing water and sodium retention that can lead to water intoxication/hyponatraemia, especially with prolonged high-dose infusion in electrolyte-free fluids. It does not typically cause peripheral neuropathy or nephrotoxicity (Kenya pharmacology guidelines; Brunner & Suddarth).
106. The cardinal movement of labor and birth in sequence is:-
A. Extension, Flexion, descent, internal rotation, restitution
B. Descent, external rotation, flexion, extension, internal rotation
C. Descent, flexion, internal rotation, extension, restitution
D. Internal rotation, descent, flexion, extension, external rotation
Answer: C. Descent, flexion, internal rotation, extension, restitution
Rationale: The cardinal movements of labour occur as: engagement, descent, flexion, internal rotation, extension, restitution (external rotation), and expulsion. Option C gives the correct ordered sequence descent, flexion, internal rotation, extension, restitution (Myles Textbook for Midwives).
107. A steady trickle of bright red blood from the vagina in the presence of firm fundus suggests: -
A. Uterine atony
B. Perineal hematoma
C. Infections of uterus
D. Lacerations of genital tract
Answer: D. Lacerations of genital tract
Rationale: Bright red bleeding with a well-contracted (firm) fundus indicates the bleeding is from a genital tract laceration, not from the uterus. Uterine atony causes a soft/boggy fundus; the firm fundus rules it out and points to trauma/laceration (Kenya EmONC; Myles).
108. Combined oral contraceptive pills are associated with :-
A. Menorrhagia
B. Polymenorrhoea
C. Ovarian cyst
D. Intermenstrual bleeding.
Answer: D. Intermenstrual bleeding.
Rationale: Combined oral contraceptive pills commonly cause breakthrough/intermenstrual bleeding (spotting), especially in the first few cycles. They typically REDUCE menstrual flow (treat menorrhagia) rather than cause it, so the associated side effect among the options is intermenstrual bleeding (Kenya National Family Planning Guidelines).
109. Skeletomuscular system after birth of a baby involves:-
A. Subsequent growth occurs by hyperplasia rather than hypertrophy
B. The long bones completely ossify
C. The bones of the vault of the skull reveal ossification
D. The muscles are complete and growth occurs by hypertrophy rather than hyperplasia
Answer: D. The muscles are complete and growth occurs by hypertrophy rather than hyperplasia
Rationale: At birth the full complement of muscle fibres is present; postnatal muscle growth occurs by hypertrophy (enlargement of existing fibres) rather than hyperplasia (increase in number). Reference: Kozier Fundamentals / Anatomy & Physiology of the newborn.
110. Preventive measures of ophthalmia neonatorum include:-
A. Administering systemic penicillin within one hour after birth, isolation of the baby
B. Instillation of penicillin eye drops after birth, taking eye swab for culture and sensitivity
C. Treatment of abnormal vaginal discharge prenatally, application of 1% tetracycline eye ointment within one hour of birth
D. Treatment of all vaginal discharges prenatally, application of 3% tetracycline eye ointment within one hour of birth
Answer: C. Treatment of abnormal vaginal discharge prenatally, application of 1% tetracycline eye ointment within one hour of birth
Rationale: Prevention of ophthalmia neonatorum combines antenatal treatment of maternal STIs/abnormal discharge with prophylactic 1% tetracycline eye ointment applied within one hour of birth. Reference: Kenya EmONC/newborn guidelines, Myles Textbook for Midwives.
111. Physiological anemia is best defined as:-
A. Hemoglobin less than 7g/dl
B. Hemodilution as a result of fluid retention
C. Low hemoglobin as a result of increase in Foetal requirements
D. Increase in plasma volume greater than the red blood cells
Answer: D. Increase in plasma volume greater than the red blood cells
Rationale: Physiological (dilutional) anaemia of pregnancy results because plasma volume rises proportionally more than the red cell mass, lowering the haemoglobin concentration by haemodilution. Reference: Myles Textbook for Midwives.
112. In Erb’s palsy:-
A. There is damage to all the brachial plexus nerve roots
B. There is damage to the facial nerves
C. There is damage to the lower brachial plexus involving the 7 th and 8 th cervical root nerves
D. There is damage to the upper brachial plexus involving the 5 th &amp; 6 th cervical root nerves
Answer: D. There is damage to the upper brachial plexus involving the 5 th &amp; 6 th cervical root nerves
Rationale: Erb's palsy results from injury to the upper brachial plexus (C5–C6 roots), classically from excessive lateral neck traction during shoulder dystocia, producing the 'waiter's tip' posture. Reference: Myles Textbook for Midwives.
113. Hypoplasia & yellow discoloration of the primary teeth has occurred in infants whose pregnant mothers were treated with drug:-
A. Penicillin.
B. Streptomycin.
C. Dihydrostretomycin.
D. Tetracycline.
Answer: D. Tetracycline.
Rationale: Tetracyclines cross the placenta, chelate calcium and deposit in developing teeth, causing enamel hypoplasia and permanent yellow-brown discolouration; they are contraindicated in pregnancy. Reference: Kenya Pharmacology/Therapeutic guidelines, Brunner & Suddarth.
114. In FANC (Focused Antenatal Care) the activities that should be done during the first visit are:-
A. Listen to fetal heart rate sound, give albendazole dose, iron, folate, counsel and educate the mother.
B. Take history, give tetanus toxoid, iron and folate, screen for syphilis
C. Perform vaginal examination, check fetal presentation, give sulfadoxine pyrimethamine dose, iron and folate
D. Give first sulfadoxine pyrimethamine dose, iron and folate, check individual birth plan, counsel and educate the mother
Answer: D. Give first sulfadoxine pyrimethamine dose, iron and folate, check individual birth plan, counsel and educate the mother
Rationale: The FANC first visit includes commencing IPTp with sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine, iron and folate supplementation, developing an individual birth plan, and counselling/health education. Reference: Kenya FANC/MOH ANC guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
115. The function of the surfactant factor production in the premature is:-
A. Decreases permeability of the alveoli
B. Provides transport for oxygen to enter the blood supply
C. Maintains the alveoli open during expiration
D. Dilates bronchioles decreasing airway resistance
Answer: C. Maintains the alveoli open during expiration
Rationale: Surfactant lowers alveolar surface tension, preventing alveolar collapse (atelectasis) at end-expiration; its deficiency in prematurity causes respiratory distress syndrome. Reference: Kenya Basic Paediatric Protocols, Anatomy & Physiology.
116. Aisha is using a childbirth strategy that includes a detailed anatomy and physiology course during pregnancy and a special breathing technique to control pushing in the final stages of labour. She is using which method?
A. Natural childbirth
B. The Lamaze method
C. Caesarean delivery
D. The Read method
Answer: B. The Lamaze method
Rationale: The Lamaze (psychoprophylaxis) method combines antenatal education on anatomy and physiology with controlled/patterned breathing techniques to manage labour and pushing. Reference: Myles Textbook for Midwives.
117. A major cardiac change that occurs in pregnancy is:-
A. Decrease in blood volume by 35%.
B. Increase in total peripheral resistance.
C. Increase in cardiac output by 40%.
D. Decrease in cardiac output by 10%
Answer: C. Increase in cardiac output by 40%.
Rationale: Cardiac output rises by about 30–40% in pregnancy due to increased stroke volume and heart rate, while total peripheral resistance falls and blood volume increases. Reference: Myles Textbook for Midwives.
118. The term used to describe the close physical contact between parents and their newborn shortly after birth is:-
A. Imprinting.
B. Early stimulation.
C. Bonding.
D. Attachment
Answer: C. Bonding.
Rationale: Bonding describes the close early physical/emotional contact from parent to newborn shortly after birth; attachment is the reciprocal enduring tie developing over time. Reference: Myles Textbook for Midwives, Kozier Fundamentals.
119. Principles of management of a woman with postpartum haemorrhage are:-
A. Rub up a contraction, give an oxytocic, resuscitate the mother
B. Call a doctor, stop the bleeding, resuscitate the mother
C. Give an oxytocic, call a doctor, stop bleeding
D. Deliver the placenta immediately, rub a contraction, give an oxytocic
Answer: A. Rub up a contraction, give an oxytocic, resuscitate the mother
Rationale: Per Kenya EmONC/Myles, the immediate management of PPH follows: rub up a contraction (stimulate the uterus to contract), administer an oxytocic to sustain contraction, and resuscitate the mother. This addresses the commonest cause (atonic uterus) first while restoring circulation.
120. Patients with high risk pregnancy should have:-
A. Follow-up in ANC every 6 weeks
B. Fetal kick chart.
C. Fetal maternal transfusion
D. Fetal biophysical profile.
Answer: D. Fetal biophysical profile.
Rationale: High-risk pregnancies require intensified fetal surveillance; the biophysical profile (combining ultrasound assessment of movement, tone, breathing, amniotic fluid and a non-stress test) is the comprehensive antenatal tool used to assess fetal wellbeing, per Kenya ANC/EmONC guidelines and Myles. Six-weekly follow-up is inadequate and the kick chart is only a basic screen.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
121. The correct sequence in resuscitating a baby at birth is: -
A. Cardiac Message, warmth and stimulation, maintenance of patent airway
B. Warmth and stimulation, maintenance of patent airway, cardiac message
C. Cardiac message, maintenance of patent airway, warmth and stimulation
D. Warmth and stimulation, oxygen administration, cardiac message
Answer: B. Warmth and stimulation, maintenance of patent airway, cardiac message
Rationale: Neonatal resuscitation per Kenya Basic Paediatric Protocols/HBB follows the sequence: provide warmth and stimulation (dry, stimulate), maintain a patent airway (position and clear/ventilate), and only then chest compressions (cardiac massage) if the heart rate remains low. Circulation support comes last after airway and breathing.
122. Observations a midwife carries on a baby undergoing phototherapy include:-
A. Skin color, vomiting, half-hourly temperature checks
B. Skin color, signs of dehydration, oedema
C. Signs of dehydration, oedema, vomiting
D. Diarrhoea, oedema, skin turgor
Answer: B. Skin color, signs of dehydration, oedema
Rationale: During phototherapy the midwife monitors skin colour (progress of jaundice), signs of dehydration (increased insensible fluid loss and loose stools), and oedema. Temperature is monitored but not half-hourly; option B best captures the standard observations per Kenya newborn care/Myles.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
123. A 29 year old primigravida woman at 16 weeks of gestation reports irritability, palpitation and difficulty in gaining weight. Physical examination is normal except for a pulse of 104 beats/min and lid lag. Laboratory studies reveal Hb of 10.1g/dl, serum free thyroxin of 10 ng/dl and thyrotropin of 0.1 mU1ow. What is the most likely diagnosis?
A. Thyroid storm.
B. Hypothyroidism.
C. Depression.
D. Hyperthyroidism.
Answer: D. Hyperthyroidism.
Rationale: Irritability, palpitations, tachycardia (104 bpm), lid lag and difficulty gaining weight with a raised free thyroxine and suppressed thyrotropin (TSH 0.1) are diagnostic of hyperthyroidism. It is not a thyroid storm as there is no hyperpyrexia or crisis. Reference: Brunner & Suddarth; Kenya maternal medicine guidelines.
124. Areas of concern in the individual birth plan during antenatal care include:-
A. Danger signs in pregnancy, identifying a birth companion, rest and exercise.
B. Identifying signs of true labour, ensuring availability of funds, ensuring availability of balanced diet.
C. Identifying a birth companion, knowing when baby is due, identifying a skilled birth attendant.
D. Planning for transport, use of prescribed drugs, knowing when baby is due.
Answer: A. Danger signs in pregnancy, identifying a birth companion, rest and exercise.
Rationale: The individual birth plan in Kenya focused ANC includes recognition of danger signs in pregnancy, identifying a birth companion, and health messages on rest and exercise, alongside skilled birth attendance, transport and finances. Option A best reflects the birth-preparedness/complication-readiness content per Kenya ANC guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
125. After fertilization, the placenta is completely formed and functioning by;
A. Option 1
B. 10 th week.
C. 12 th week.
D. 16 th week.
Answer: C. 12 th week.
Rationale: The placenta is completely formed and fully functioning by the end of the 12th week (end of the first trimester), after which it takes over hormone production from the corpus luteum. Reference: Myles Textbook for Midwives.
