A. Involves subjecting instruments to hot air for oxidation of microbes
B. Uses a combination of heat and high pressures to sterilize instruments
C. Kills bacteria in vegetative form only
D. Is not as effective as high level disinfection (HLD)
Answer: B. Uses a combination of heat and high pressures to sterilize instruments
Rationale: Autoclaving uses moist heat (saturated steam) under high pressure to achieve sterilization, killing all microorganisms including spores. It is superior to high-level disinfection, and destroys both vegetative forms and spores, per Kenya IPC guidelines.
3. The following nursing theorist proposed that professional growth is from novice to expert;
A. Virginia Henderson
B. Sr. Calista Roy
C. Hildegard Peplau
D. Patricia Benner
Answer: D. Patricia Benner
Rationale: Patricia Benner's 'From Novice to Expert' model describes five stages of professional skill acquisition (novice, advanced beginner, competent, proficient, expert). This is standard nursing theory content (Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing).
4. The following statement is included in Orem’s theory of nursing:-
A. Self-perception
B. Love and belonging
C. Physiologic needs
D. Maintenance of a sufficient intake of air
Answer: D. Maintenance of a sufficient intake of air
Rationale: Orem's Self-Care Deficit Theory lists universal self-care requisites, the first being maintenance of a sufficient intake of air (plus water and food). Self-perception, love/belonging and physiologic needs belong to Maslow's hierarchy, not Orem. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing; nursing theory syllabus.
5. A nurse is caring for a patient with a do-not-resuscitate (DNR) order. What should the nurse prioritize when providing care to this patient:-
A. Administering cardiopulmonary resuscitation (CPR) if the patient’s condition worsens
B. Discussing resuscitation preferences with the patients family
C. Implementing comfort measures and respecting the patient wishes
D. Notifying the physician to remove the DNR order
Answer: C. Implementing comfort measures and respecting the patient wishes
Rationale: A DNR order means no CPR; nursing priority is comfort, dignity and honouring the patient's autonomous wishes. Administering CPR or seeking to remove the order would violate the directive. Reference: NCK Code of Ethics; palliative care guidelines.
6. A situation in which the nurse experiences a conflict with personal values or beliefs and some aspect of patient care is
A. Bioethical conflict
B. Ethical dilemma
C. Ethical distress
D. Ethical conduct
Answer: C. Ethical distress
Rationale: Ethical (moral) distress occurs when the nurse's personal values or beliefs conflict with an aspect of patient care, producing discomfort. Kozier Fundamentals distinguishes this from an ethical dilemma, which is a conflict between two or more competing but equally defensible courses of action requiring a choice. Because the stem describes a values conflict with patient care rather than a choice between two right actions, the answer is ethical distress.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
7. Nursing assessment during care plan development differs from medical assessment in that:-
A. Nursing assessment focuses on patient responses to actual problems
B. Medical assessment focuses on community health
C. Medical assessment focuses on health promotion
D. Nursing assessment diagnoses pathological illness
Answer: A. Nursing assessment focuses on patient responses to actual problems
Rationale: Nursing assessment focuses on the patient's human responses to actual or potential health problems (nursing diagnoses), whereas medical assessment focuses on diagnosing the disease/pathology. This distinction is core to the nursing process in Kozier Fundamentals.
8. The pillar of infection control associated with breaking the chain of infection at the reservoir stage is:-
A. Hand washing
B. Patient placement
C. Prudent use of antibiotics
D. Personal protective equipment
Answer: C. Prudent use of antibiotics
Rationale: The reservoir is where the infectious agent lives and multiplies; breaking the chain at the reservoir includes controlling the source such as prudent/rational antibiotic use to prevent resistant organism reservoirs and treating the source. Hand washing and PPE address the mode of transmission/portals rather than the reservoir per Kenya IPC guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
9. System model theory was formulated by:-
A. Dorothy Johnson
B. Virginia Henderson
C. Imogene King
D. Jean Watson
Answer: A. Dorothy Johnson
Rationale: The Behavioral System Model was developed by Dorothy Johnson, who conceptualised the person as a behavioural system of interrelated subsystems. Imogene King developed the Goal Attainment theory and Jean Watson the theory of Human Caring. Per standard nursing theory texts.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
10. A nurse encounters a situation where a patient is upset and requests a specific medication, but the nurse believes it is not in the patient’s best interest. What is the nurse’s primary responsibility in this situation?
A. Administering the medication as requested
B. Discussing the situation with the charge nurse
C. Explaining the reasons for the decision to the patient
D. Refusing the patients request without explanation
Answer: C. Explaining the reasons for the decision to the patient
Rationale: The nurse's primary responsibility is to uphold beneficence and non-maleficence while respecting patient autonomy through honest communication; this is achieved by explaining the clinical reasons for withholding the requested medication. Administering something not indicated, or refusing without explanation, breaches professional ethics. Per NCK Code of Ethics and Conduct.
11. Defining characteristics that are included in Nursing diagnosis should:-
A. Precede problem statement
B. Include treatment plans
C. Follow the etiology statement
D. Include negative statements
Answer: C. Follow the etiology statement
Rationale: In a NANDA nursing diagnosis (problem related to etiology as evidenced by defining characteristics), the defining characteristics (signs/symptoms) follow the etiology statement. They are not treatment plans and should not precede the problem statement (Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing).
12. A reflective reasoning process that guides a nurse in generating, implementing and evaluating approaches for dealing with client care and professional concerns:-
A. Nursing process
B. Critical thinking
C. Practice act
D. Nursing care plan
Answer: B. Critical thinking
Rationale: Critical thinking is the reflective reasoning process guiding the nurse to generate, implement and evaluate approaches to client care and professional concerns. The nursing process is the systematic framework applied, while critical thinking is the underlying cognitive reasoning skill (Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing).
13. Perinatal mortality refers to:-
A. Number of stillbirths per 1,000 total births.
B. Number of stillbirths & neonatal deaths per 1,000 total births.
C. Number of stillbirths & neonatal deaths per 1,000 live births.
D. Number of neonatal deaths per 1,000 total births.
Answer: B. Number of stillbirths & neonatal deaths per 1,000 total births.
Rationale: The perinatal mortality rate is the number of stillbirths plus early neonatal deaths per 1,000 total births (live births plus stillbirths). Reference: Kenya MOH reproductive health indicators; WHO definitions.
Year: 2026
14. Foods that may predispose a client to megaloblastic anemia if taken exclusively include:
A. Meat, eggs and beans
B. Beans, Peas and rice
C. Capsicum , Sorghum and Millet
D. Meat, Maize, Sorghum
Answer: B. Beans, Peas and rice
Rationale: Megaloblastic anaemia results from folate and/or vitamin B12 deficiency. A diet based exclusively on beans, peas and rice (largely staple/legume, minimal animal-source foods) lacks adequate bioavailable vitamin B12, predisposing to megaloblastic anaemia. Meat, eggs and beans (option A) actually supply B12. Per Kenya nutrition guidelines and Brunner & Suddarth haematology.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
15. Appropriate nursing interventions for a patient with “Risk for infection related to altered immunity response” include: -
A. Barrier nursing, avoiding invasive procedures and inspection of entry point sites for pathogens
B. Reverse barrier nursing, monitor SPO2 hourly and serve high protein diet
C. Isolation nursing, obtaining cultures & sensitivities daily and administration of antibiotics
D. Reverse barrier nursing, avoiding invasive procedures & daily assessment of intravenous sites
Answer: D. Reverse barrier nursing, avoiding invasive procedures & daily assessment of intravenous sites
Rationale: For an immunocompromised patient (altered immunity), protective/reverse barrier nursing shields the patient from external pathogens, avoiding invasive procedures reduces portals of entry, and daily inspection of IV sites detects early infection. This is the most complete protective-precautions set. Per Kenya IPC guidelines and Kozier.
16. Orem’s self-care model depicts nursing as: -
A. Performing self-care activities for a patient to enhance recovery.
B. A profession that is centred on client Self-care activities .
C. Assisting individuals to their optimal level of self-care.
D. Promoting environment- client interactivity to enhance healing
Answer: C. Assisting individuals to their optimal level of self-care.
Rationale: Orem's Self-Care Deficit Theory frames nursing as assisting individuals to meet their self-care needs and attain their optimal level of self-care independence. Nursing acts only when a self-care deficit exists (Kozier, nursing theory).
17. The indications for terminal cleaning of isolation room include: -
A. Infective hepatitis, meningococcal hepatitis, malaria, HIV infection
B. Chicken pox, malaria, measles and pneumonia
C. Measles, chicken pox, infective hepatitis
D. Methicillin resistant staphylococcus aureus (MRSA), liver cirrhosis, measles & hepatitis
Answer: C. Measles, chicken pox, infective hepatitis
Rationale: Terminal (thorough post-discharge) cleaning is indicated for rooms that housed communicable infections such as measles, chicken pox and infective hepatitis. Options mixing non-infectious conditions like liver cirrhosis or malaria are incorrect (Kenya IPC guidelines).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
Year: Undated
18. A client tells the nurse that the television newscaster is sending a secret message to her. The nurse suspects the client is experiencing;
A. A delusion
B. Flight of ideas
C. Ideas of reference
D. A hallucination
Answer: C. Ideas of reference
Rationale: When a client believes that neutral external events, such as a newscast, carry a special personal message directed at them, this is an idea of reference. It differs from a fixed false belief (delusion) in that it specifically involves misinterpreting external stimuli as personally significant. Kozier/Psychiatric Nursing terminology.
19. A patient has been taking antipsychotic drugs for years. You notice that he begins to grind his teeth, moves and smacks his lips frequently. These features are suggestive of;
A. Acute dystonia
B. Gustatory hallucinations
C. Oculogyric crisis
D. Tardive dyskinesia
Answer: D. Tardive dyskinesia
Rationale: Long-term antipsychotic use causes tardive dyskinesia, characterised by involuntary repetitive orofacial movements such as lip smacking, teeth grinding and tongue movements. Acute dystonia and oculogyric crisis are early extrapyramidal effects, not late ones. Kenya Pharmacology/Psychiatric Nursing guidelines.
20. Which nursing action is best when trying to diffuse a client’s impending violent behavior?
A. Placing the client in seclusion
B. Leaving the client alone until the client can talk about feelings
C. Involving the client in a quiet activity to divert attention
D. Helping the client identify and express feelings of anxiety and anger
Answer: D. Helping the client identify and express feelings of anxiety and anger
Rationale: The least restrictive, most therapeutic de-escalation approach is to help the client identify and verbalise underlying feelings of anxiety and anger before they escalate to violence. Seclusion is a last resort and diversion alone does not address the emotion. Psychiatric Nursing de-escalation principles.
21. Which of the following concepts would a nurse emphasize when conducting a community education programme on reducing the risk of rape?
A. Rape rarely occurs in rural areas
B. The very young people are safe from rape
C. People who walk in groups are less likely to be raped
D. Rape is a response to sexual need
Answer: C. People who walk in groups are less likely to be raped
Rationale: Rape prevention education emphasises risk-reduction facts, including that being in a group offers greater safety than being alone. The other statements are myths: rape occurs in all settings and age groups and is an act of power/aggression, not sexual need. Community/Mental Health Nursing.
22. One among these leadership approaches emphasis the value of hierarchy for enhancing organizational effectiveness;
A. Transactional Theory.
B. Transformational Theory.
C. Contingencies Theory.
D. Situations Theory.
Answer: A. Transactional Theory.
Rationale: Transactional leadership relies on established hierarchy, structure, rewards and corrective actions to achieve organizational effectiveness. Transformational leadership by contrast inspires beyond hierarchy. Nursing Management & Leadership references.
23. A client admitted for alcohol detoxification develops increased tremors, irritability, hypertension and fever. The nurse should be alert for impending?
A. Korsakoff’s syndrome
B. Esophageal varices
C. Wernicke’s syndrome
D. Delirium tremens
Answer: D. Delirium tremens
Rationale: Increasing tremors, irritability, hypertension, tachycardia and fever during alcohol withdrawal signal impending delirium tremens, a life-threatening state occurring within 24-72 hours. Wernicke's and Korsakoff's are thiamine-deficiency syndromes with different presentations. Psychiatric Nursing.
24. An honest leader is known by:
A. Transparent vision and ambition.
B. Being capable of taking decisions.
C. Effective planning.
D. Collaboration.
Answer: A. Transparent vision and ambition.
Rationale: Honesty in leadership is demonstrated through transparency, sharing a clear vision and being open about intentions and ambition. The other options describe competence, planning and teamwork rather than the integrity trait of honesty. Nursing Leadership references.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
25. The following are signs of suicide tendencies
A. Expressing suicidal ideas, eating adequate food
B. Appearing peaceful and happy, adequate sleep
C. Writing farewell letters, appearing happy
D. Expressing suicidal ideas, Writing farewell letters
Answer: D. Expressing suicidal ideas, Writing farewell letters
Rationale: Clear warning signs of suicide risk include openly expressing suicidal ideation and writing farewell/goodbye letters or giving away possessions. The other options pair a true sign with distractors like adequate eating/sleeping/appearing happy that do not reliably indicate risk. Psychiatric Nursing assessment.
26. Sexual arousal by wearing clothes of opposite sex is called:
A. fortteurism
B. Fertishism
C. Transvestism
D. Exhibitionism
Answer: C. Transvestism
Rationale: Transvestism (transvestic disorder) is sexual arousal derived from wearing clothing of the opposite sex. Fetishism involves arousal from objects, exhibitionism from exposing oneself, and frotteurism from touching a non-consenting person. Psychiatric Nursing/DSM-based texts.
27. A series of either structured or unstructured questions to assess suitability for recruitment is:
A. Testing.
B. Interview.
C. Hiring.
D. Evaluation.
Answer: B. Interview.
Rationale: An interview is a series of structured or unstructured questions used to assess a candidate's suitability for recruitment. Testing measures specific abilities, hiring is the act of employment, and evaluation is appraisal of performance. Nursing Management/Human Resource references.
28. Bases for ethical behavior includes:
A. Belief, retribution, concept, attitude, principles.
B. Retribution, value, belief, concept, principle.
C. Belief, value, attitude, concept, principle.
D. Retribution, undoing, concept, value, principle.
Answer: C. Belief, value, attitude, concept, principle.
Rationale: Ethical behaviour is grounded in beliefs, values, attitudes, concepts and principles rather than retribution or undoing (which are punishment/defense concepts). Per Kozier Fundamentals and NCK professional ethics content, these are the recognised bases of ethical conduct.
29. The most effective leadership style, that can be used during an emergency situation is?*
A. Laissez -faire.
B. Autocratic.
C. Democratic.
D. Supportive.
Answer: B. Autocratic.
Rationale: In an emergency, rapid unilateral decision-making is required, so the autocratic (authoritarian) style is most effective as it allows the leader to give direct orders without lengthy consultation. This is standard nursing leadership and management doctrine.
30. An example of a gender identity disorder is;
A. Voyeurism
B. Fetishism
C. Transsexualism
D. Transvestism
Answer: C. Transsexualism
Rationale: Transsexualism is a gender identity disorder (gender dysphoria), reflecting incongruence between experienced and assigned gender. Voyeurism, fetishism and transvestism are paraphilias (sexual disorders), not gender identity disorders.
31. A young man is remanded by the court for psychiatric treatment. His police record, which dates to his early teenage years includes delinquency, running away, auto theft and vandalism. He dropped out of school at age 16 and has been living on his own since then. His history suggests maladaptive coping, which is associated with;
A. Obsessive compulsive disorder
B. Narcissistic personality disorder
C. Antisocial personality disorder
D. Borderline personality disorder
Answer: C. Antisocial personality disorder
Rationale: A pattern of delinquency, running away, theft, vandalism and disregard for rules and social norms beginning before age 15 is the classic conduct-disorder history that progresses to antisocial personality disorder per DSM/psychiatric nursing criteria.
32. A managerial function that indicates leading the staff in the most applicable method is:
A. Controlling.
B. Planning.
C. Directing.
D. Organizing.
Answer: C. Directing.
Rationale: Directing (leading) is the management function that guides and leads staff to carry out work using the most applicable methods. Controlling monitors, planning sets goals, and organizing structures resources.
33. A client is admitted with a diagnosis of delusion of grandeur. This diagnosis reflects a belief that one is;
A. Highly important or famous
B. Responsible for the evil in the world
C. Connected to events unrelated to self
D. Being persecuted
Answer: A. Highly important or famous
Rationale: Delusion of grandeur is a fixed false belief of exaggerated self-importance, power, wealth or fame. Persecution reflects paranoid delusions, guilt reflects nihilistic/depressive delusions, and unrelated events reflect delusions of reference.
34. A joint trait of a leader that defines his ability to posses’ honesty, responsibility maturity in the working area?
A. Personality.
B. flexibility
C. Integrity.
D. Intelligence.
Answer: C. Integrity.
Rationale: Integrity is the leadership trait defined by honesty, responsibility and maturity, uniting these qualities in the workplace. Personality, flexibility and intelligence are separate traits.
35. A hypothesis which a researcher tries to disprove is:
A. Research hypothesis
B. Null hypothesis
C. Positive hypothesis
D. Alternate hypothesis
Answer: B. Null hypothesis
Rationale: The null hypothesis (H0) states no relationship/difference and is the statement the researcher seeks to reject or disprove through statistical testing. The research/alternate hypothesis is what the researcher hopes to support.
36. The best literature in academic terms is a: -
A. Scientific publication
B. Recent book
C. Conference proceedings
D. Refereed journal article
Answer: D. Refereed journal article
Rationale: A refereed (peer-reviewed) journal article is regarded as the best academic literature because it undergoes expert scrutiny before publication, ensuring credibility and currency. Books and conference proceedings rank lower in the evidence/academic hierarchy.
37. Nurse asks a patient to remember three words: garden, house and rain. About 10 minutes later, she asks the patient to repeat those words. Which aspect of memory is the nurse testing?
A. Immediate memory
B. Recent memory
C. Remote memory
D. Delayed memory
Answer: B. Recent memory
Rationale: Asking the patient to recall three words after a short delay of a few minutes tests recent (short-term) memory. Immediate memory is instant repetition, remote memory covers past life events, and delayed recall at minutes still falls under recent memory in the mental status exam.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
38. The following is an advantage of close ended questions in a questionnaire are advantageous in that : -
A. They are easy to construct
B. They Permit greater depth of response
C. They are easy to administer
D. Their responses are limited
Answer: D. Their responses are limited
Rationale: Closed-ended questions restrict respondents to predetermined answer options, which makes them easy to code and analyse but limits the range of responses. Among the choices, 'their responses are limited' is the true characteristic/advantage of closed questions (they yield standardised, quantifiable data). Per Kozier and nursing research methodology texts.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
39. A leader who is mainly concerned with day today operations of an organization with little regard to the future is called.
A. Traditional leader.
B. Charismatic leader.
C. Transformational leader.
D. Transactional leader.
Answer: D. Transactional leader.
Rationale: A transactional leader focuses on day-to-day operations, maintaining routines and exchanges (rewards/punishments) with little vision for future change, unlike the transformational leader who inspires future-oriented change. Per nursing leadership and management references.
40. Illusion is
A. Misinterpretation of actual stimuli
B. Sensory distortions
C. False perception
D. Increased intensity of stimuli
Answer: A. Misinterpretation of actual stimuli
Rationale: An illusion is a misperception or misinterpretation of a real (actual) external stimulus, distinguishing it from a hallucination (perception without any stimulus). Per standard psychiatric nursing texts.
41. The contraindications of Electroconvulsive therapy include;
A. Organic psychosis
B. Schizophrenia
C. Affective disorders
D. Psychosis associated with pregnancy and childbirth
Answer: A. Organic psychosis
Rationale: ECT is indicated for severe depression, catatonia, some schizophrenia and puerperal psychosis, but is contraindicated/risky in organic brain conditions (e.g., raised intracranial pressure, organic psychosis) due to cerebral compromise. The other listed conditions are indications, not contraindications. Per psychiatric nursing references.
42. Operant conditioning refers to;
A. Learning that is repeated.
B. Learning that is skill based.
C. Learning that is attitude based.
D. Learning that is facilitated through reinforcement.
Answer: D. Learning that is facilitated through reinforcement.
Rationale: Operant conditioning (Skinner) is learning in which behaviour is shaped and maintained by its consequences—reinforcement increases and punishment decreases the likelihood of a behaviour recurring. Per psychology/behavioural learning theory in nursing curriculum.
43. Features of antisocial personality disorder include;
A. Disregard for the rights of others, exploit others for material gain, able to tolerate frustration
B. Unable to tolerate frustration, able to anticipate negative effects of their actions, dishonesty
C. Dishonesty, disregard for the feeling of others, exploit others for personal gratification
D. Exploit others for personal gratification, regard for the feelings of others, motivated by punishment
Answer: C. Dishonesty, disregard for the feeling of others, exploit others for personal gratification
Rationale: Antisocial personality disorder is characterised by pervasive disregard for and violation of the rights/feelings of others, deceitfulness (dishonesty), exploitation of others, impulsivity, low frustration tolerance and failure to learn from punishment. Option C captures these features accurately, whereas the other options contain incorrect elements such as 'regard for feelings' or 'able to anticipate negative effects.' Per DSM/psychiatric nursing texts.
44. An exaggerated concern with one’s self-image especially with personal appearance that mounts to psychiatric symptoms include;
A. Obsession
B. Histrionic
C. Compulsion
D. Narcissism
Answer: D. Narcissism
Rationale: Narcissism denotes an exaggerated, excessive concern with self-image, self-importance and personal appearance/admiration that, when pathological, constitutes a psychiatric symptom (narcissistic personality disorder). Per psychiatric nursing references.
45. The best description of Division of work is:
A. Work should be divided into small tasks.
B. It leads to specialization.
C. Labour is divided according to specialization
D. Resource should be divided.
Answer: C. Labour is divided according to specialization
Rationale: Fayol's principle of division of work means labour is divided among workers according to their specialization so each performs the task they do best, thereby increasing efficiency. This best describes the principle itself, while 'leads to specialization' is a result. Per management/leadership references.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
46. Identifying the patient problem is a part of:
A. Implementation
B. Nursing assessment
C. Nursing diagnosis
D. Planning
Answer: C. Nursing diagnosis
Rationale: In the nursing process, identifying the patient's actual or potential health problem is the essence of the nursing diagnosis phase, which follows assessment (data collection). Per Kozier Fundamentals of Nursing.
47. Which of the following is not a type of quantitative research?
A. Descriptive research
B. Experimental
C. Quasi experimental
D. Ethnography research
Answer: D. Ethnography research
Rationale: Ethnography is a qualitative research design that studies cultures and social groups through immersion and observation; it is not quantitative. Descriptive, experimental and quasi-experimental designs are all quantitative. Per nursing research methodology.
48. When research findings can be applied to the larger population
A. Study generalization
B. Internal validity
C. External validity
D. Study reliability
Answer: C. External validity
Rationale: External validity refers to the degree to which research findings can be generalized and applied beyond the study sample to the larger target population and other settings. This is distinct from internal validity, which concerns causal accuracy within the study (Polit & Beck; NCK research syllabus).
49. Over delegation involves;
A. Transferring too much roles from the manager to the subordinates.
B. Delegation at the wrong time, to the wrong person for the long reasons.
C. Delegating to exceptionally competent employees.
D. Delegating authority and responsibility.
Answer: A. Transferring too much roles from the manager to the subordinates.
Rationale: Over-delegation is the error of transferring excessive tasks, authority, or roles from the manager to subordinates, overburdening them beyond their capacity. It is one of the common delegation errors alongside under-delegation and improper delegation (Nursing Management & Leadership, NCK).
50. A scale of measurement with an absolute zero include: -*
A. Interval
B. Nominal
C. Ratio
D. Ordinal
Answer: C. Ratio
Rationale: The ratio scale is the only measurement scale possessing a true/absolute zero point, allowing meaningful ratios (e.g., weight, height, pulse rate). Interval scales lack an absolute zero (Research & Biostatistics, NCK syllabus).
51. The characteristic feature of paranoid personality disorder is;
A. Lifelong social withdrawal and discomfort with human interaction
B. Dependence on others for emotional support and reassurance
C. Intense and chaotic relationships
D. Long standing suspiciousness and mistrust for people
Answer: D. Long standing suspiciousness and mistrust for people
Rationale: Paranoid personality disorder is characterized by a pervasive, long-standing pattern of distrust and suspiciousness of others, interpreting their motives as malevolent. Social withdrawal is schizoid, dependence is dependent PD, and chaotic relationships suggest borderline PD (DSM-5; Kenya Mental Health nursing).
52. One of the following antipsychotic drugs has a higher likelihood of causing extrapyramidal side effects;
A. Olanzapine
B. Haloperidol
C. Quetiapine
D. Resperidone
Answer: B. Haloperidol
Rationale: Haloperidol is a high-potency typical (first-generation) antipsychotic with the greatest likelihood of causing extrapyramidal side effects. Olanzapine, quetiapine and risperidone are atypicals with lower EPS risk (Kenya Pharmacology/Therapeutic guidelines; Mental Health nursing).
53. Mental health is defined as
A. A state of well-being where a person can realize his abilities to cope with normal stress of life
B. It the promotion of health prevention of mental disorder
C. Ability to distinguish what is real from what is not
D. Absence of mental illness.
Answer: A. A state of well-being where a person can realize his abilities to cope with normal stress of life
Rationale: WHO defines mental health as a state of well-being in which an individual realizes their own abilities, can cope with the normal stresses of life, work productively and contribute to their community. It is more than the mere absence of illness (WHO; Kenya Mental Health policy).
54. A cooperative approach to teaching where two or more teachers take part in planning, teaching and evaluating a group of students is;
A. Critical incident technique
B. Case study technique
C. Project teaching
D. Team teaching
Answer: D. Team teaching
Rationale: Team teaching is a cooperative approach in which two or more teachers jointly plan, deliver and evaluate instruction for the same group of students. This distinguishes it from individual techniques like case study or project teaching (Nursing Education methods, NCK).
55. In order to admit a foreigner suffering from mental disorder in a mental hospital in Kenya, the government concerned would apply in writing to;
A. Medical practitioners and dentist board
B. Mental health boards
C. Cabinet secretary for health
D. Director of medical services
Answer: D. Director of medical services
Rationale: Under the Kenyan Mental Health Act, admission of a foreigner with mental disorder requires the concerned government to apply in writing to the Director of Medical Services, who authorizes reception into a mental hospital. This is the statutory authority for such reception orders (Mental Health Act, Kenya).
56. The main neurotransmitter involved in causation of schizophrenia is?
A. Glutamate levels
B. Dopamine
C. GABA
D. Acetylcholine
Answer: B. Dopamine
Rationale: The dopamine hypothesis is the principal neurotransmitter theory of schizophrenia, attributing positive symptoms to dopaminergic hyperactivity in the mesolimbic pathway; this underpins the action of antipsychotics as dopamine antagonists (Kenya Mental Health nursing; Pharmacology).
57. Master K aged 14years is brought to the psychiatric unit by the father with allegations of being violent and lack of insight. The likely mode of admission is;
A. Observation
B. Emergency
C. Voluntary
D. Involuntary
Answer: D. Involuntary
Rationale: A 14-year-old brought against his will by the father, presenting as violent with lack of insight (unable to consent), is admitted involuntarily under the Mental Health Act on application by a relative. Voluntary admission requires the patient's own consent/insight, which is absent here (Mental Health Act, Kenya).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
58. In Erikson`s theory of personality development identity crisis and role confusion mark the period of:
A. Middle adulthood
B. Adolescence
C. Old age
D. Early adulthood
Answer: B. Adolescence
Rationale: In Erikson's psychosocial stages, identity vs. role confusion is the crisis of adolescence (roughly 12-18 years), when the young person forms a stable sense of self. This is standard developmental theory in Kozier Fundamentals and NCK mental health curriculum.
59. One of the general principles of psychiatric nursing is:
A. Use self-understanding as a therapeutic tool
B. Judgment of patient symptoms
C. Repeated talking
D. Reassuring the patient
Answer: A. Use self-understanding as a therapeutic tool
Rationale: A core principle of psychiatric nursing is therapeutic use of self, which requires self-awareness/self-understanding. The nurse should accept the patient non-judgmentally rather than judge symptoms or give false reassurance, per NCK mental health nursing standards.
60. You have been asked by your instructor to discuss about contingency Theory. What should you include in your discussion?
A. It emphasizes that both leaders and followers should act on one another to raise their motivation.
B. It states that leadership qualities inspire followers to be motivated. By what they do.
C. It is a theory in leadership that views the pattern of a leader’s behavior as dependent on the interaction of the personality and the needs of the situation.
D. It considers that leaders must provide followers the sense of security and approval and discipline to succeed in the output.
Answer: C. It is a theory in leadership that views the pattern of a leader’s behavior as dependent on the interaction of the personality and the needs of the situation.
Rationale: Contingency theory holds that effective leadership depends on matching the leader's style/personality to situational demands; there is no single best style. Option A describes transformational and D describes other models, so C is correct per nursing management/leadership references.
61. The reliability of a measuring tool has following aspects, except:
A. internal consistency
B. efficiency
C. stability
D. equivalence
Answer: B. efficiency
Rationale: Reliability of a measurement tool encompasses stability (test-retest), internal consistency (homogeneity), and equivalence (inter-rater/parallel forms). Efficiency is not an attribute of reliability, making it the exception per research methods texts.
62. The lowest level of learning in affective domain is?
A. Valuing
B. Receiving
C. Attending
D. Organization
Answer: B. Receiving
Rationale: In Krathwohl's affective domain hierarchy, Receiving (attending/awareness) is the lowest level, followed by responding, valuing, organization and characterization. Receiving is the correct lowest level as a taxonomy category.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
63. Statistical parameter commonly used to estimate sample size include: -
A. Standard deviation
B. Median
C. T-score
D. Level of Confidence
Answer: D. Level of Confidence
Rationale: Sample size estimation formulas require the confidence level (e.g., 95%, Z-value), the margin of error, and an estimate of variability. Among the options, level of confidence is a statistical parameter directly used in sample size calculation, per research methods.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
64. The following psychiatric treatments require that a psychiatric nurse obtain written informed consent?
A. Electroconvulsive therapy
B. Behavioral therapy
C. Supportive psychotherapy
D. Group therapy
Answer: A. Electroconvulsive therapy
Rationale: ECT is an invasive procedure with significant risks and therefore legally requires written informed consent. Behavioural, supportive and group psychotherapy are talking therapies not requiring separate written consent, per psychiatric nursing law and ethics.
65. The statement that best describes participative leadership:
A. Supportive and withdrawn.
B. Supportive and relational.
C. Enabling and controlling.
D. Enabling and supportive.
Answer: D. Enabling and supportive.
Rationale: Participative (democratic) leadership empowers/enables followers by involving them in decision-making while remaining supportive. It is not controlling or withdrawn, so 'enabling and supportive' best describes it per nursing leadership references.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
66. A patient with delusional disorder may require which type of medication to stabilize aggressive behavioral disturbance?
A. Sedative
B. Antidepressants
C. Neuroleptics
D. Antiparkinsonism
Answer: C. Neuroleptics
Rationale: Delusional disorder is treated with antipsychotics (neuroleptics), which also control associated aggressive/behavioural disturbance. Antidepressants and antiparkinsonism agents do not target delusions or aggression, per psychiatric pharmacology.
67. Regarding interpretation of Inferential statistics: -
A. When the P value is more than the Alpha value (α) we accept the alternative hypothesis
B. When the P value is more than the Alpha value (α) we reject the null hypothesis
C. When the P value is less than the Alpha value (α) we accept the null hypothesis
D. When the P value is less than the Alpha value (α) we reject the null hypothesis
Answer: D. When the P value is less than the Alpha value (α) we reject the null hypothesis
Rationale: In hypothesis testing, when p < α the result is statistically significant, so the null hypothesis is rejected in favour of the alternative. This is the standard rule of inferential statistics.
68. Q The right sequence of subgroups in cognitive domains is;
A. Knowledge, comprehension, application, synthesis, analysis evaluation
B. Knowledge, comprehension, application, evaluation, analysis, synthesis
C. Knowledge, comprehension, evaluation, application, analysis, synthesis
D. Knowledge, comprehension, application, analysis synthesis, evaluation
Answer: D. Knowledge, comprehension, application, analysis synthesis, evaluation
Rationale: Bloom's cognitive domain taxonomy proceeds from lowest to highest order: knowledge, comprehension, application, analysis, synthesis, and evaluation. This is the classic sequence used in NCK curriculum objective writing (educational psychology references).
69. In qualitative research, a guiding principle in deciding sample size is: -
A. Sub-group analysis
B. Number of variables
C. Data saturation
D. Effect size
Answer: C. Data saturation
Rationale: In qualitative research, sample size is guided by data saturation, the point at which no new themes or information emerge from additional data collection. Effect size and sub-group analysis relate to quantitative studies (Polit & Beck research methods).
70. Which of the following classification of research designs is based on the dimension of control over independent variables?
A. Structured and flexible
B. Experimental/quasi experimental and non-experimental
C. Retrospective and prospective
D. Cross-sectional and longitudinal
Answer: B. Experimental/quasi experimental and non-experimental
Rationale: Classifying designs by the degree of control/manipulation over the independent variable yields experimental (full control with manipulation and randomisation), quasi-experimental (partial control), and non-experimental designs. Retrospective/prospective and cross-sectional/longitudinal classify by time, not control.
71. "Above all, do no harm". This principle is encompassed in the ethical dimension of:
A. Non-maleficence
B. Anonymity
C. Human dignity
D. Beneficence
Answer: A. Non-maleficence
Rationale: 'Above all, do no harm' (primum non nocere) is the ethical principle of non-maleficence, the duty to avoid causing harm. Beneficence is the separate duty to do good (nursing ethics, Kozier Fundamentals).
72. The father of scientific management is:
A. Tom peters.
B. Edwards Deming.
C. Henri Gantt.
D. Frederick W. Taylor.
Answer: D. Frederick W. Taylor.
Rationale: Frederick Winslow Taylor is universally recognised as the father of scientific management, having developed time-and-motion principles for efficiency. Deming is associated with quality management and Gantt with scheduling charts.
73. The management theorist who felt that the interrelationships between the groups leader and its members were most influenced by the managers ability to be a good leader is?
A. Mc Gregor.
B. Mary Follett.
C. Burns.
D. Fiedler.
Answer: D. Fiedler.
Rationale: Fiedler's contingency model of leadership holds that group effectiveness depends on the interaction (match) between the leader's style and the favourableness of the situation, including leader-member relations. This is the theorist emphasising the manager's leadership ability in relation to group members (management/leadership theory).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
74. This change will be mostly resisted if:
A. It is started when there are major changes going on in the organization.
B. Wide opportunities are provided for nurses to express both negative and positive concerns on primary nursing
C. The benefits are widespread beyond the nursing services.
D. Staff themselves created it and already understand it too well.
Answer: A. It is started when there are major changes going on in the organization.
Rationale: Change is most strongly resisted when it is introduced during a period of other major organisational upheaval, as staff are already overwhelmed and threatened. Involvement, widespread benefit, and staff ownership all reduce resistance (nursing management, planned change theory).
75. A researcher selects a ________ and uses inferential statistics to draw conclusions about the larger ________.
A. sample; population
B. parameter; population
C. population; sample
D. sample; statistic
Answer: A. sample; population
Rationale: A researcher selects a sample and uses inferential statistics to generalise findings to the larger population from which the sample was drawn. This is the defining logic of inferential statistics (Polit & Beck).
76. The primary purpose of conducting a literature review is: -
A. To summarize the findings of a research study
B. To generate new research questions
C. To provide background information on the research topic
D. To present the results of data analysis
Answer: C. To provide background information on the research topic
Rationale: The primary purpose of a literature review is to provide background, establish what is already known, identify gaps, and situate the study within existing knowledge. Summarising a single study's findings or presenting data analysis are not its role.
77. A Cross-sectional research is: -
A. The collection of data from more than one case at one moment in time
B. A comparison of two or more variables for a long period of time
C. One that is devised when the researcher is in a bad mood
D. A study of one particular section of society e.g. the middle classes
Answer: A. The collection of data from more than one case at one moment in time
Rationale: A cross-sectional design collects data from many cases/participants at a single point in time to describe or compare variables. It contrasts with longitudinal designs that follow subjects over time (research methods, Polit & Beck).
78. Regarding patient classification system (PCS)?*
A. It is a method of grouping patients according to length or duration of nursing care.
B. It is a measuring tool used to describe the progress of patient’s status
C. It depends on the complexity of nursing care requirements of the patient.
D. All of the above
Answer: D. All of the above
Rationale: A patient classification system (PCS) groups patients by the duration and complexity of nursing care required, and serves as a tool describing the acuity/status of patients for staffing decisions. All three descriptions are correct features of PCS per nursing management/leadership texts (Kozier; NCK management syllabus).
79. Drug dependence means
A. Experiencing psychotic symptoms
B. Maladaptive pattern of substance use
C. Developing complications for drugs
D. Physiological and psychological dependence on drugs
Answer: D. Physiological and psychological dependence on drugs
Rationale: Drug dependence is a state in which the body physiologically and/or psychologically requires the substance to function, distinguished from abuse/maladaptive use. This is the classic definition in psychiatric nursing and Kenya mental health/pharmacology references.
80. In depression there is a deficiency of?
A. Acetylcholine
B. GABA
C. 5-HT
D. Dopamine
Answer: C. 5-HT
Rationale: Depression is associated with a deficiency of serotonin (5-HT), along with noradrenaline; the monoamine hypothesis underpins SSRI therapy. This is standard psychiatric/pharmacology teaching (Brunner & Suddarth; NCK mental health syllabus).
81. An unknown African adult man who has been wandering around Kasarani and throwing stones to the passersby has been arrested by the area chief and presented to Mathari hospital for psychiatric assessment. The psychiatrist recommends admission. Which admission form will the chief fill?
A. MOH 614
B. MOH 637
C. MOH 638
D. MOH 613
Answer: D. MOH 613
Rationale: Under the Kenya Mental Health Act, an unknown/vagrant person requiring involuntary psychiatric admission via a chief/administrative order is admitted on MOH 613 (reception order for a person of unsound mind found wandering). The chief completes this form as the referring authority.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
82. Which among these statements gives a false description of a vision?
A. It is projected with a broad time frame.
B. It reflects what the organization wants to be
C. It uses action word in present tense.
D. It reflects why the organization exists.
Answer: D. It reflects why the organization exists.
Rationale: A vision statement describes the desired future state of the organization (what it wants to become), is future/broad-time-framed, and inspires. Describing 'why the organization exists' is the purpose of a MISSION statement, so this is the false description of a vision (NCK management/leadership).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
83. An instructor records the average grade on an exam in her class. What type of statistics is average:
A. Parameter
B. Descriptive
C. Professional
D. Inferential
Answer: B. Descriptive
Rationale: An average (mean) computed to summarize the data of the class is a descriptive statistic, since it describes/summarizes the observed sample rather than inferring beyond it. A parameter describes a whole population, and this is a sample of one class (research & statistics).
84. An interval scale: -
A. Is the most informative scale of measurement
B. Has no true zero
C. Both a and b
D. Is distributed on an equidistant scale
Answer: B. Has no true zero
Rationale: An interval scale has equal, meaningful intervals but no true (absolute) zero point (e.g., temperature in Celsius). The most informative scale is the ratio scale, not interval, so 'has no true zero' is the defining feature here (research & statistics).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
85. ________________ is NOT a component of HMIS
A. Financial Management System (FMS)
B. Electronic Health Records (EHR)
C. Customer Relationship Management (CRM)
D. Clinical Decision Support System (CDSS)
Answer: C. Customer Relationship Management (CRM)
Rationale: HMIS components in health include EHR, clinical decision support (CDSS) and financial management. Customer Relationship Management (CRM) is a business/marketing system, not a component of a Health Management Information System (Kenya HMIS/health informatics).
86. A delusion in which the patient believes that others, oneself or the world do not exist:
A. Secondary delusion
B. Bizarre delusion
C. Persecutory delusions
D. Nihilistic delusion
Answer: D. Nihilistic delusion
Rationale: A nihilistic delusion is the false belief that oneself, others, or the world does not exist or is dead/destroyed (as in Cotard syndrome). This matches the described belief exactly (psychiatric nursing / mental health syllabus).
87. A biased sampling method include: -
A. Quota
B. Stratified random
C. Cluster
D. Systematic
Answer: A. Quota
Rationale: Quota sampling is a non-probability method in which the researcher selects subjects to fill quotas, introducing selection bias. Stratified random, cluster and systematic sampling are probability methods designed to minimize bias (research methods).
88. The primary goal of HMIS include: -
A. Managing human resources in healthcare facilities
B. Tracking medical research and development
C. Regulating healthcare policies and procedures
D. Improving patient care and health outcomes
Answer: D. Improving patient care and health outcomes
Rationale: The Health Management Information System (HMIS) exists to generate quality data that supports clinical decision-making, planning and monitoring, with the ultimate goal of improving patient care and health outcomes. Per Kenya MOH HMIS/DHIS2 policy and nursing management texts, data collection is a means, not the end.
89. Deductive Reasoning is applied in:
A. Quantitative research
B. Applied research
C. Action research
D. Qualitative research
Answer: A. Quantitative research
Rationale: Deductive reasoning moves from general theory to specific hypothesis testing, which characterises quantitative research. Qualitative/action research typically employ inductive reasoning (specific observations to broader theory), per standard nursing research texts used in the NCK research syllabus.
90. A health care service programme is most sustainable if:
A. High percentage of recurrent budget is funded by donors.
B. High percentage of recurrent budget is funded by clients.
C. High percentage of capital budget is funded by the donor.
D. High percentage of recurrent budget is financed by government.
Answer: D. High percentage of recurrent budget is financed by government.
Rationale: Sustainability is greatest when recurrent (ongoing operating) costs are financed by a stable domestic source, i.e. government, rather than donor funds which are time-limited and unpredictable. This aligns with Kenya health financing and sustainability principles in health systems management.
91. The following is a condition associated with high risk of suicide:
A. Hypomania
B. Chronic schizophrenia
C. Major depression
D. Drug abuse
Answer: C. Major depression
Rationale: Major depression carries the highest risk of completed suicide among psychiatric conditions due to hopelessness, worthlessness and suicidal ideation. Per Kozier/psychiatric nursing references, depression is the condition most strongly associated with suicide risk.
92. The term schizophrenia was coined by: -
A. Philippe Pinel
B. Eugene Bleula
C. William Tuke
D. Benjamin Rush
Answer: B. Eugene Bleula
Rationale: The term schizophrenia was coined by Eugen Bleuler in 1908, replacing Kraepelin's 'dementia praecox'. The option 'Eugene Bleula' is the intended (misspelled) reference to Eugen Bleuler, per standard psychiatric nursing history.
93. Qualities that best defines a leader who uses laissez-faire includes:
A. Foster independence in team by promoting motivation and creativity.
B. The group in planning and decision making.
C. Provide little autonomy and self-motivation
D. Passive and puts the responsibility of decision making on others.
Answer: D. Passive and puts the responsibility of decision making on others.
Rationale: Laissez-faire (hands-off) leadership is characterised by a passive leader who delegates decision-making to the group and provides minimal direction. Per nursing leadership/management texts, this best defines the laissez-faire style.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
94. One of the following conditions would be well suited to use nursing critical pathway:
A. Bacterial infection acquired in a foreign country.
B. Foreign object in the ear.
C. Fever of unknown origin.
D. Hip replacement surgery.
Answer: D. Hip replacement surgery.
Rationale: Critical (clinical) pathways work best for high-volume, predictable conditions with a well-defined, standardised course of care such as elective hip replacement. Conditions of unknown origin or unpredictable course are poorly suited, per nursing management references.
95. The following are examples of negative symptoms of schizophrenia
A. Avolition and delusions
B. Alogia and anhedonia
C. Hallucinations and alogia
D. Disorganized behavior and avoition.
Answer: B. Alogia and anhedonia
Rationale: Negative symptoms of schizophrenia are deficits: avolition, alogia, anhedonia, affective flattening and asociality. Only option B lists two true negative symptoms; the others contain positive symptoms (delusions, hallucinations, disorganized behavior).
96. Projected teaching aids include,
A. Video, white board
B. Television, overhead projector
C. Flipcharts, pictures
D. Writing boards, handouts
Answer: B. Television, overhead projector
Rationale: Projected teaching aids require projection equipment/light to display images. Television and overhead projector are projected aids, whereas flipcharts, pictures, writing boards, white boards and handouts are non-projected. Per educational technology principles in nursing education.
97. Q39Nursing intervention that is appropriate for a patient who is suicidal is:
A. Teach healthier problem-solving skills
B. Ignore patient’s suicidal comments
C. Report to the unit doctor
D. Reassure the patient that suicidal thoughts will be reduced
Answer: C. Report to the unit doctor
Rationale: For an actively suicidal patient the priority nursing intervention is ensuring safety, which includes reporting/notifying the treating team so protective measures (constant observation, removal of harmful objects) are instituted. Ignoring comments and false reassurance are both inappropriate, and teaching problem-solving skills is a later therapeutic step once immediate safety is secured (psychiatric/mental health nursing standards). The single most appropriate immediate action among the options is reporting to the unit doctor.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
98. Decision making hierarchy is called;
A. Scalar chain.
B. Span of control.
C. Centralized decision making.
D. Decentralized decision making.
Answer: A. Scalar chain.
Rationale: The scalar chain refers to the line of authority and the hierarchy of decision making from top to bottom in an organization, per management principles (Fayol). Span of control is the number of subordinates a manager oversees, not the decision hierarchy.
99. Which of the following best suits a patient exhibiting flight of ideas?
A. Provides excessive details
B. Talk excessively while frequently shifting from one idea to another
C. Make abrupt stops in the flow of conversation
D. Coining new words
Answer: B. Talk excessively while frequently shifting from one idea to another
Rationale: Flight of ideas is a thought process disturbance seen in mania where speech is rapid and continuous with abrupt shifts between loosely connected ideas. This matches excessive talk with frequent topic shifting, per Kaplan & Sadock psychiatry.
100. Regarding competency-based curriculum;
A. Assumes that nursing is a series of discrete independent disciplines,
B. Carried out by subject specialists, who determine the subdivision of content instruction
C. Identifying professional competencies required & teaching to achieve competencies in a specified setting.
D. Combined in a meaningful way disciplined knowledge to impart wholesome learning
Answer: C. Identifying professional competencies required & teaching to achieve competencies in a specified setting.
Rationale: A competency-based curriculum is built around identifying required professional competencies and structuring teaching/learning to achieve those competencies. This aligns with the NCK competency-based education framework.
101. The power gained through knowledge and experience is called:
A. Legitimate power.
B. Reward power.
C. Expert power.
D. Referent power.
Answer: C. Expert power.
Rationale: Expert power is derived from an individual's knowledge, skills and experience, per French and Raven's bases of power. Legitimate power comes from position, reward from ability to give benefits, and referent from personal attributes/admiration.
102. An objective is referred to as specific because;
A. It is covered after a whole instructional entity.
B. Content that is repeated
C. It is classified.
D. It addresses a very specific area of content to be covered.
Answer: D. It addresses a very specific area of content to be covered.
Rationale: In SMART objectives, 'specific' means the objective targets a clearly defined, narrow area of content or behaviour to be achieved, per curriculum/education principles.
103. Which of the following statements most accurately describes the role of exclusion criteria in clinical trials?
A. Exclusion criteria help in determining the study's statistical power by controlling the sample size and adjusting for potential biases in data analysis.
B. Exclusion criteria are primarily focused on increasing the generalizability of the study findings by including a diverse participant population with varying characteristics.
C. Exclusion criteria are used to identify participants who meet the inclusion criteria for the study, ensuring that the sample is representative of the target population.
D. Exclusion criteria are applied to prevent participants with certain characteristics from entering the study to minimize confounding variables and ensure the safety of participants.
Answer: D. Exclusion criteria are applied to prevent participants with certain characteristics from entering the study to minimize confounding variables and ensure the safety of participants.
Rationale: Exclusion criteria specify characteristics that disqualify potential participants, protecting participant safety and reducing confounding to improve internal validity of the trial, per research methodology. Inclusion criteria (not exclusion) define who qualifies for the study.
104. The most lethal side effect of antipsychotic drugs is;
A. Neuroleptic malignant syndrome
B. Oculogyric crisis
C. Tardive dyskinesia
D. Gustatory hallucinations
Answer: A. Neuroleptic malignant syndrome
Rationale: Neuroleptic malignant syndrome is a rare but life-threatening reaction to antipsychotics characterized by hyperthermia, muscle rigidity, autonomic instability and altered consciousness, and is the most lethal adverse effect. Tardive dyskinesia and oculogyric crisis are distressing but not typically fatal.
105. A theorist who proposed that learning is more meaningful when the outcome is made clear is;
A. Watson
B. Skinner
C. Bandura
D. Thorndike
Answer: D. Thorndike
Rationale: Thorndike's laws of learning, particularly the law of effect, emphasize that learning is strengthened when outcomes/consequences are clear and satisfying. This links meaningful learning to clarity of outcome, per educational psychology.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
106. Creation of new words which are not in any vocabulary by a psychiatric patient is called;
A. Circumstantiality
B. Neologism
C. Illusion
D. Delusion
Answer: B. Neologism
Rationale: Neologism is the coining of new words that have no meaning in standard vocabulary, a language disturbance seen in psychosis such as schizophrenia, per Kaplan & Sadock. Circumstantiality is over-inclusion of detail, illusion is misperception, and delusion is a false fixed belief.
107. Secondary prevention of mental illness include;
A. Targeting and diminishing stressors within the environment
B. Early identification of problems and prompt initiation of effective treatment
C. Reducing residual defects associated with severe mental illness
D. Assisting individuals to increase their ability to cope effectively with stress
Answer: B. Early identification of problems and prompt initiation of effective treatment
Rationale: Secondary prevention aims at early detection and prompt treatment to reduce duration and severity of illness. Reducing stressors is primary prevention, while reducing residual defects is tertiary prevention, per community mental health levels of prevention.
108. One of the following clients would most likely be selected for case management;
A. A 41 – year old client admitted for outpatient tonsillectomy.
B. An adolescent with gunshot wound he is in the Emergency room (ER).
C. A young adult with a fractured pelvis.
D. An elderly client awaiting hip replacement.
Answer: B. An adolescent with gunshot wound he is in the Emergency room (ER).
Rationale: Case management targets high-cost, high-acuity, complex clients whose care must be coordinated across the continuum. A trauma victim (gunshot wound) in the ER represents the unpredictable, resource-intensive, multidisciplinary case that most benefits from case management, unlike the routine, predictable outpatient/elective cases. This reflects nursing management principles in NCK leadership curricula (Kozier).
109. leadership style used to maintain a strong control in the department;
A. Autocratic.
B. Laissez -faire.
C. Collegial.
D. Democratic.
Answer: A. Autocratic.
Rationale: The autocratic (authoritarian) leadership style centralises decision-making and maintains strong, tight control within the department, with the leader directing and retaining authority. This contrasts with laissez-faire (hands-off), democratic and collegial (participative) styles. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals and NCK nursing management/leadership content.
110. Which of the following behaviors would indicate the greatest improvement in a patient who was admitted to the hospital with a diagnosis of hyperactivity?
A. Frequently apologies for his behavior
B. Shows interest in other patients in the ward.
C. Completes assigned task
D. Takes naps during the day
Answer: C. Completes assigned task
Rationale: In hyperactivity (e.g., mania/ADHD-type presentation), the therapeutic goal is improved attention span, follow-through and the ability to focus and finish activities. Completing an assigned task demonstrates restored concentration and impulse control, the clearest marker of improvement. Reference: NCK mental health nursing curriculum.
111. The following is a goal of teaching;
A. To involve pupils in activities
B. To impart knowledge
C. To share experiences
D. To entice learners
Answer: B. To impart knowledge
Rationale: The fundamental goal of teaching is to impart knowledge (and skills/attitudes) to learners, producing a change in behaviour. Involving pupils and sharing experiences are methods/activities, not the core goal, and enticing learners is not a legitimate aim. Reference: NCK nursing education principles.
112. In multi-stage sampling, how are clusters typically selected?
A. Using stratified random sampling
B. Using simple random sampling
C. Using convenience sampling
D. Using systematic sampling
Answer: B. Using simple random sampling
Rationale: In multistage sampling, clusters (primary sampling units) are typically selected by simple random sampling at each stage, giving each cluster a known equal probability of selection. Reference: NCK research and biostatistics methodology.
113. Drug of choice in the management of alcohol withdrawal is:
A. Benzodiazepines
B. Disulfiram
C. Antidepressants
D. Acamprosate
Answer: A. Benzodiazepines
Rationale: Benzodiazepines (e.g., diazepam, chlordiazepoxide) are the drugs of choice for managing acute alcohol withdrawal, preventing seizures and delirium tremens. Disulfiram and acamprosate are used for relapse prevention/maintenance abstinence, not acute withdrawal. Reference: Kenya clinical/mental health guidelines and NCK pharmacology.
114. Educational assessment that is carried out at the end of the course is;
A. Summative assessment
B. Formative assessment
C. Criterion assessment
D. Semester assessment
Answer: A. Summative assessment
Rationale: Summative assessment is conducted at the end of a course/programme to judge overall achievement and assign grades, whereas formative assessment is ongoing to guide learning. Reference: NCK nursing education and assessment principles.
115. The primary objective of experimental methodology is:
A. To reduce ethical problems
B. Improve internal validity
C. Ensure external validity
D. To eliminate type I error
Answer: B. Improve internal validity
Rationale: Experimental designs, through randomisation and control of confounding variables, primarily aim to establish cause-and-effect relationships, i.e., to maximise internal validity. External validity (generalisability) is typically stronger in other designs. Reference: NCK research methodology.
116. Regarding sample size determination: -
A. When the study population is less than 10, 000 sample size is adjusted using Fischer’s formula
B. When the study population is more than 10, 000 sample size is adjusted using Fischer’s formula
C. When the study population is more than 10, 000 sample size is adjusted using Cochran’s formula
D. When the study population is less than 10, 000 only the Cochran’s formula is used to calculate the sample size
Answer: A. When the study population is less than 10, 000 sample size is adjusted using Fischer’s formula
Rationale: For populations under 10,000, the Fisher's formula sample size is adjusted (finite population correction) to obtain the final sample size, while for populations of 10,000 or more the unadjusted Fisher's formula is used. This is the standard convention taught in NCK research methodology.
117. A centralized schedule is controlled by?
A. Departmental Nurse
B. Unit Supervisor.
C. Chief Nurse.
D. Ward In charge.
E. Fo
Answer: C. Chief Nurse.
Rationale: In centralised scheduling, one central authority, the chief nurse (nursing director/office), controls and prepares staffing schedules for all units, ensuring consistency and equitable resource distribution. Decentralised scheduling is controlled at unit level by the ward in-charge/supervisor. Reference: NCK nursing management/leadership.
118. The role of a teacher in innovative learning methods is that;
A. The teacher controls every stage of learning directly
B. The teacher functions mainly as a learning facilitator
C. The teacher focuses primarily on factual transmission
D. The teacher minimizes learner participation activities
Answer: B. The teacher functions mainly as a learning facilitator
Rationale: In innovative, learner-centred (constructivist) teaching methods, the teacher's role shifts from director/transmitter of facts to a facilitator who guides and supports active learner participation. Options describing teacher control, factual transmission and minimised participation reflect traditional teacher-centred methods. Per NCK curriculum principles of teaching-learning methodology.
119. A micro-teaching skill intended to capture learners’ attention at the beginning of a lesson is;
A. Reinforcement through positive learner encouragement
B. Stimulus variation through movement and gestures
C. Set induction through structured lesson introduction
D. Closure through summarization of major points
Answer: C. Set induction through structured lesson introduction
Rationale: Set induction is the micro-teaching skill used at the beginning of a lesson to gain and focus learners' attention and prepare them for what follows, per NCK nursing education/teaching methodology. Reinforcement, stimulus variation and closure serve other stages of the lesson.
120. Majority of people who complete suicide have a pre- existing psychiatric illness. From history taking, the following has been found to be the most common illness;
A. Conduct disorder.
B. Chronic anxiety
C. Major depression.
D. Schizophrenic disorders.
Answer: C. Major depression.
Rationale: Major depressive disorder is the psychiatric illness most commonly associated with completed suicide, per Kenya mental health guidelines and standard psychiatric nursing texts. Schizophrenia, anxiety and conduct disorder carry lower attributable risk than depression.
121. A nurse educator wants learners to independently identify learning gaps, search for resources, and later synthesize solutions during tutorials. The teaching strategy that best supports this approach is;
A. Problem Based Learning emphasizing self-directed inquiry
B. Demonstration method emphasizing guided observation
C. Lecture method emphasizing content transmission
D. Seminar method emphasizing prepared presentations
Answer: A. Problem Based Learning emphasizing self-directed inquiry
Rationale: Problem-Based Learning has learners identify their own learning gaps, seek resources and synthesise solutions around a problem, matching self-directed inquiry per NCK/nursing education methodology. Lecture, demonstration and seminar are more teacher- or presentation-driven.
122. An appropriate description of attention is;
A. Ability to focus on a particular item.
B. Ability to sustain one’s focus on an item.
C. Ability to recall activities in the immediate past.
D. Ability to concentrate on a particular activity
Answer: A. Ability to focus on a particular item.
Rationale: Attention is the ability to focus on a particular stimulus or item, per psychiatric nursing/psychology definitions; sustaining that focus over time is concentration/vigilance. Recall of immediate past events describes memory, so option A best defines attention.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
123. A client experiences intense fear whenever she sees dogs after being bitten during childhood. A behavioral principle that best explains development of this phobia is;
A. Operant conditioning
B. Classical conditioning
C. Cognitive restructuring
D. Observational learning
Answer: B. Classical conditioning
Rationale: A phobia developing after a dog bite is a learned association between a previously neutral stimulus (dog) and an aversive event (bite), explained by classical (Pavlovian) conditioning, per psychiatric nursing/behavioural theory. Operant conditioning involves reinforcement of behaviour, observational learning is modelling, and cognitive restructuring is a treatment technique.
124. A teaching method is suitable for generating many possible solutions to a community health problem within a short time is;
A. Brainstorming encouraging unrestricted idea contribution
B. Tutorial encouraging guided reflective interaction
C. Demonstration encouraging procedural observation
D. Role play encouraging experiential re-enactment
Answer: A. Brainstorming encouraging unrestricted idea contribution
Rationale: Brainstorming is the teaching/group method designed to generate a large number of ideas or possible solutions quickly by encouraging free, uncensored contribution without immediate criticism. This aligns with community health participatory problem-solving methods in the NCK community health curriculum and Kozier fundamentals.
125. A researcher investigates whether nurses working night shifts experience higher fatigue levels compared to nurses working day shifts. The researcher does not manipulate any variables but examines existing differences. The study method being used in this case is;
A. Historical research analyzing records from previous institutions
B. True experimental research using controlled intervention methods
C. Causal-comparative research examining possible existing causes
D. Descriptive research exploring characteristics without comparisons
Answer: C. Causal-comparative research examining possible existing causes
Rationale: When groups (night vs day shift) that already differ are compared and the researcher does not manipulate any variable, the design is causal-comparative (ex post facto). It examines existing differences to infer possible causes, unlike true experimental research which requires manipulation and control. This is standard nursing research methodology.
126. During systematic desensitization therapy, the nurse should first assist the patient to:
A. Keep off all situations associated with the feared stimulus
B. Analyze unconscious conflicts causing irrational fear reactions
C. Confront the feared object immediately without preparation sessions
D. Develop relaxation techniques before gradual exposure procedures
Answer: D. Develop relaxation techniques before gradual exposure procedures
Rationale: Systematic desensitization is a behavioral therapy that begins by teaching the patient relaxation techniques, then constructs an anxiety hierarchy and pairs relaxation with gradual, graded exposure to the feared stimulus. Relaxation training is the essential first step. This aligns with standard psychiatric/mental health nursing practice.
127. While selecting a method of teaching, a teacher chooses role play in teaching conflict resolution in nursing leadership. The rationale for the choice of the method is that;
A. Role play promotes experiential attitude development
B. Role play simplifies memorization of leadership terms
C. Role play reduces learner participation demands
D. Role play emphasizes passive observation skills
Answer: A. Role play promotes experiential attitude development
Rationale: Role play is an affective-domain, experiential method ideal for teaching interpersonal skills, attitudes and processes like conflict resolution, because learners actively enact and reflect on situations. This makes it the appropriate rationale for its selection in leadership teaching.
128. A psychiatric patient with aggression will be present with;
A. Stomping away from the nurses’ station, going to another room, and grabbing a snack from another patient.
B. Bursting into tears, leaving the community meeting, and sitting on a bed hugging a pillow.
C. Telling the primary nurse, I felt angry when you said I could not have a second helping at lunch.
D. Telling the medication nurse, I am not going to take that or any other medication you try to give me.
Answer: A. Stomping away from the nurses’ station, going to another room, and grabbing a snack from another patient.
Rationale: Aggression is behaviour that violates others' rights/boundaries, here shown by stomping off and taking another patient's snack. Bursting into tears reflects sadness, and verbally stating anger appropriately or refusing medication assertively are not aggression. This reflects psychiatric nursing assessment of aggressive behaviour.
129. While managing a child with severe acute malnutrition, you carefully initiate feeds in small frequent amounts. The rationale for this intervention is to;
A. To prevent fluid overload and metabolic imbalance
B. To encourage rapid weight gain immediately
C. To reduce nursing workload during admission
D. To eliminate the need for intravenous fluids
Answer: A. To prevent fluid overload and metabolic imbalance
Rationale: In severe acute malnutrition, small frequent feeds (F-75 in the stabilization phase) prevent refeeding syndrome, fluid overload, heart failure and metabolic/electrolyte imbalances. This aligns with the Kenya IMAM guidelines and WHO SAM management protocol.
130. A function of teacher that involves helping learners identify alternatives and make informed decisions regarding learning is;
A. Assessment through competency measurement
B. Counseling through learner support guidance
C. Planning through activity organization
D. Communication through content explanation
Answer: B. Counseling through learner support guidance
Rationale: Helping learners identify alternatives and make informed decisions about their learning is the counseling/guidance function of the teacher, providing support rather than assessing, planning or merely communicating content. This reflects the teacher roles in the NCK nurse-educator framework.
131. A researcher states the hypothesis: “There is no relationship between nurses’ workload and patient satisfaction scores.” The type of hypothesis stated is;
A. Null hypothesis denying existence of a relationship
B. Complex hypothesis involving several independent variables
C. Directional research hypothesis predicting positive association
D. Alternative hypothesis suggesting significant variable differences
Answer: A. Null hypothesis denying existence of a relationship
Rationale: A statement asserting 'there is no relationship' between variables is the null hypothesis (H0), which denies any association or difference. It is neither directional, complex, nor the alternative hypothesis. This is basic nursing research/statistics knowledge.
132. The type of variables in research that has a potential to influence the outcome of the research and are either unnoticed or uncontrolled are:
A. Extraneous
B. Dependent
C. Independent
D. Confounding
Answer: A. Extraneous
Rationale: Extraneous variables are those that are unnoticed or uncontrolled yet can influence the study outcome; a confounding variable is a specific extraneous variable that is recognised and related to both the independent and dependent variables. Since the stem stresses 'unnoticed or uncontrolled', the general category is extraneous variables (Polit & Beck; Kozier research chapter).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
133. A parent has come to the clinic with a 5-year-old boy with history of attacks of frightening with imaginations of snake crawling over him and he is acting as trying to remove it. On assessment the child is cooperative and quite intelligent. The most appropriate explanation for the child behavior is;
A. Night terror.
B. Bipolar disorder.
C. Acute phobic hallucination.
D. Delusional infestation.
Answer: C. Acute phobic hallucination.
Rationale: A cooperative, intelligent 5-year-old with transient frightening visual experiences (seeing snakes crawling and trying to remove them) reflects acute phobic hallucinations of childhood, a benign self-limiting phenomenon rather than a psychotic or mood disorder. Delusional infestation and bipolar disorder are adult psychiatric conditions, and night terrors occur in sleep with no recall (standard child psychiatry references).
134. An experimental design that has a pretest and a post test for both control and experimental groups, does not assign subjects to the groups at random and only gives the intervention to the experimental group is;
A. Solomon 4 group
B. Post-test only control group
C. Pretest-posttest control group
D. Non-equivalent control group
Answer: D. Non-equivalent control group
Rationale: A design with pretest and post-test for both control and experimental groups but WITHOUT random assignment is the non-equivalent control group design, a quasi-experimental design. Random assignment would make it a true pretest-posttest control group design (Polit & Beck Nursing Research).
135. A nurse manager notices that postoperative infection rates have increased over the last six months despite adherence to routine protocols. She decides to conduct a systematic investigation to determine contributing factors and recommend solutions. A statement that best describes the activity the nurse is undertaking is;
A. Conducting a routine administrative audit to monitor staffing trends
B. Reviewing patient complaints to support disciplinary staff procedures
C. Applying a systematic inquiry to solve a clinical practice problem
D. Performing informal observation to improve ward communication patterns
Answer: C. Applying a systematic inquiry to solve a clinical practice problem
