Rationale: A systematic investigation to identify contributing factors and recommend solutions to a clinical problem (rising post-op infection rates) describes research/systematic inquiry applied to practice. It goes beyond a staffing audit or informal observation (Polit & Beck; NCK research principles).
136. A teacher decides to organize students into small groups where each group studies a different aspect of maternal health and later reports findings to the whole class. The teaching strategy being used is;
A. Snowball discussion emphasizing progressive interaction
B. Brainstorming emphasizing spontaneous idea generation
C. Syndicate discussion emphasizing divided group assignments
D. Panel discussion emphasizing prepared expert responses
Answer: C. Syndicate discussion emphasizing divided group assignments
Rationale: A syndicate method divides the class into small groups, each assigned a different sub-topic to study and later report back to the whole class. This matches the described strategy exactly (nursing education/teaching methods references).
137. The role played by perpetuating factors to mental illnesses is to;
A. Appear to have induced the disorder
B. Occur shortly before the onset of the disorder.
C. Prolong the disorder after it has begun.
D. Determine the person’s vulnerability to the illness.
Answer: C. Prolong the disorder after it has begun.
Rationale: In the biopsychosocial formulation, perpetuating factors are those that maintain or prolong a disorder once it has started, distinct from predisposing (vulnerability) and precipitating (onset-triggering) factors (standard psychiatric formulation; Gelder/Kaplan).
138. Kurt Schneider’s first-rank symptoms of schizophrenia include;
A. Thought broadcasting, euphoric mood, audible thoughts.
B. Depressed mood, voices discussing, thought broadcasting.
C. Audible thoughts, voices arguing, delusions.
D. Delusions, perplexity, depressed mood.
Answer: C. Audible thoughts, voices arguing, delusions.
Rationale: Schneider's first-rank symptoms include audible thoughts (thought echo), voices arguing/discussing in the third person, running commentary, thought withdrawal/insertion/broadcasting, made feelings/impulses/acts, somatic passivity and delusional perception. Mood symptoms such as euphoria/depression are NOT first-rank, eliminating the other options (standard psychiatry).
139. The intelligence quotient of a child with severe mental retardation is in the range of;
A. Below 20.
B. 20 to 34.
C. 35 to 54.
D. 55 to 70.
Answer: B. 20 to 34.
Rationale: By ICD classification of intellectual disability, severe mental retardation corresponds to an IQ of 20-34, moderate is 35-49, mild is 50-69, and profound is below 20 (ICD-10; standard psychiatry references).
140. A mechanism that explains why a child with kwashiorkor will have bilateral pitting edema is;
A. Excessive sodium loss
B. Increased magnesium level
C. Increased calcium absorption
D. Reduced plasma oncotic pressure
Answer: D. Reduced plasma oncotic pressure
Rationale: Kwashiorkor causes hypoalbuminaemia; low plasma albumin reduces plasma oncotic (colloid osmotic) pressure, allowing fluid to shift into the interstitium and producing bilateral pitting oedema. Reference: Kenya IMNCI/Basic Paediatric Protocols, Ministry of Health nutrition guidelines.
141. A researcher develops a questionnaire measuring patient satisfaction and administers it twice to the same group of patients within two weeks obtaining same results. This exercise demonstrates;
A. Inter-rater reliability between independent data collectors
B. Internal consistency reliability among questionnaire sections
C. Test-retest reliability showing stability over time periods
D. Parallel-form reliability comparing different research instruments
Answer: C. Test-retest reliability showing stability over time periods
Rationale: Administering the same instrument to the same group on two occasions and obtaining similar results demonstrates test-retest reliability, which assesses temporal stability. Reference: NCK Research & Statistics curriculum; standard nursing research methodology.
142. A medication that is most commonly used in management of acute dystonic reactions caused by antipsychotics is;
A. Fluoxetine for serotonin reuptake inhibition effects
B. Diazepam for generalized anxiety symptom reduction
C. Benztropine for anticholinergic symptom relief actions
D. Lithium for mood stabilization during psychotic episodes
Answer: C. Benztropine for anticholinergic symptom relief actions
Rationale: Acute dystonic reactions from antipsychotics are treated with anticholinergic agents such as benztropine (or diphenhydramine), which counter the extrapyramidal dopamine-acetylcholine imbalance. Reference: Kenya pharmacology/therapeutic guidelines; standard psychiatric nursing texts.
143. In lilliputian hallucinations;
A. The false perceptions occur on waking up.
B. The false perceptions occur when falling asleep.
C. The objects appear larger than in real life phenomena.
D. The objects appear smaller than in real life phenomena.
Answer: D. The objects appear smaller than in real life phenomena.
Rationale: Lilliputian hallucinations are visual hallucinations in which people or objects appear abnormally small (micropsia-like). Reference: standard psychiatric/mental health nursing texts.
144. In a learning process, a student accurately performs a skill under supervision and later combines several related skills smoothly during management of a emergency. The type of psychomotor progression is evident in the student is;
A. Precision progressing toward articulation
B. Manipulation progressing toward imitation
C. Articulation progressing toward manipulation
D. Naturalization progressing toward precision
Answer: A. Precision progressing toward articulation
Rationale: In the psychomotor (Simpson/Dave) domain, performing a skill accurately under supervision reflects precision, and smoothly coordinating several skills together reflects articulation; thus the learner progresses from precision to articulation. Reference: NCK education/teaching methodology curriculum.
145. According to Freud, the latency stage is primarily characterized by:
A. Sexual maturity with establishment of intimate adult relationships
B. Focus on academic learning and peer socialization activities
C. Exploration of bodily functions and toilet training mastery
D. Dependency on oral gratification through sucking behaviors
Answer: B. Focus on academic learning and peer socialization activities
Rationale: Freud's latency stage (approximately 6 years to puberty) is characterised by dormant sexual drives with energy channelled into schoolwork, skills development and same-sex peer relationships. Reference: developmental psychology; NCK mental health/growth and development curriculum.
146. A community nurse assessing growth identifies a child whose weightforheight is significantly below expected standards but heightforage is normal. The nutritional problem the child is suffering from is;
A. Stunting
B. Wasting
C. Obesity
D. Retardation
Answer: B. Wasting
Rationale: Low weight-for-height with normal height-for-age indicates acute malnutrition, i.e. wasting; stunting would show low height-for-age. Reference: Kenya IMNCI and WHO growth standards used in MOH nutrition assessment.
147. During a lesson, a tutor asks learners to compare several teaching methods, identify strengths and limitations, and justify the most suitable option for community training. The cognitive level the tutor is assessing is;
A. Analysis through differentiation of instructional components
B. Identification through recognition of teaching characteristics
C. Application through use of familiar instructional procedures
D. Synthesis through creation of entirely new methodologies
Answer: A. Analysis through differentiation of instructional components
Rationale: Comparing methods, identifying strengths/limitations and justifying a choice requires breaking material into parts and examining relationships, which is Bloom's analysis level. Reference: Bloom's taxonomy; NCK teaching methodology curriculum.
148. During a study on medication adherence, several participants relocate and cannot be contacted for follow-up assessments. The limitation likely to occur is;
A. Attrition affecting completeness of longitudinal data collection
B. Instrument bias influencing reliability of measurement tools
C. Recall bias resulting from inaccurate participant memory
D. Researcher bias caused by subjective data interpretation
Answer: A. Attrition affecting completeness of longitudinal data collection
Rationale: Loss of participants who relocate and cannot be followed up during a longitudinal adherence study is attrition (loss to follow-up), which reduces sample completeness and threatens internal validity. Per Kozier/nursing research principles, attrition is the specific limitation when participants drop out over repeated follow-up assessments.
149. A client diagnosed with claustrophobia is most likely to experience severe anxiety when:
A. Entering enclosed spaces such as elevators or tunnels
B. Seeing blood during invasive medical treatment procedures
C. Standing near heights overlooking deep valleys and bridges
D. Encountering insects within outdoor recreational environments
Answer: A. Entering enclosed spaces such as elevators or tunnels
Rationale: Claustrophobia is the specific phobia of enclosed spaces, so severe anxiety is triggered by elevators or tunnels. Blood relates to blood-injection-injury phobia, heights to acrophobia, and insects to entomophobia (DSM-5/psychiatric nursing).
150. A key feature that distinguishes innovative teaching methods from traditional teaching methods is that;
A. Content delivery depends mainly on oral lectures
B. Assessment focuses entirely on factual recall
C. Teacher controls all learning experiences directly
D. Learners assume greater responsibility for learning
Answer: D. Learners assume greater responsibility for learning
Rationale: Innovative/learner-centred methods shift responsibility to the learner (active, self-directed learning), unlike traditional teacher-centred lecturing where the teacher controls delivery and assessment focuses on recall. This is a core curriculum/pedagogy principle in NCK nurse education.
151. A facilitator summarizes major points and emphasizes relevance before ending the lesson. The micro-teaching skill being applied is;
A. Stimulus variation through tonal adjustment
B. Closure through lesson summarization
C. Reinforcement through positive feedback
D. Set induction through attention capture
Answer: B. Closure through lesson summarization
Rationale: Summarizing major points and emphasizing relevance at the end of a lesson is the micro-teaching skill of closure. Set induction is at the start, stimulus variation involves varying stimuli, and reinforcement is feedback for responses.
152. The process of ensuring that the research participants are not aware that they belong to either experimental and control groups is;
A. Fraud
B. Placebo
C. Debriefing
D. Blinding
Answer: D. Blinding
Rationale: Keeping participants unaware of their group allocation (experimental vs control) is blinding (masking), which reduces bias. A placebo is an inert treatment, debriefing is post-study disclosure, and fraud is misconduct (nursing research methods).
153. A curriculum committee organizes content from simple concepts toward increasingly complex clinical applications. The curriculum principle reflected in this case is;
A. Curriculum continuity through repetitive reinforcement
B. Curriculum sequence through progressive complexity
C. Curriculum integration through multidisciplinary overlap
D. Curriculum flexibility through optional learning pathways
Answer: B. Curriculum sequence through progressive complexity
Rationale: Organizing content from simple to complex reflects the curriculum principle of sequence (logical ordering/progression). Continuity is repeated recurrence, integration is horizontal linking of subjects, and flexibility offers optional pathways.
154. In a research process, the degree of control that a research design exerts over the extraneous variables is;
A. Rigor
B. Validity
C. Reliability
D. Randomization
Answer: B. Validity
Rationale: The degree of control a design exerts over extraneous variables is internal validity. Rigor is overall methodological quality, reliability is consistency of measurement, and randomization is one technique used to achieve control (Kozier/nursing research).
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
155. A psychiatric nurse caring for a client diagnosed with paedophilic disorder should initially prioritize the following intervention;
A. Assessing risk for harm toward vulnerable child populations
B. Exploring childhood memories without discussing legal implications
C. Promoting independent community living without family supervision
D. Encouraging unrestricted interaction with neighborhood children regularly
Answer: A. Assessing risk for harm toward vulnerable child populations
Rationale: Safety is the priority in psychiatric nursing; for paedophilic disorder the nurse must first assess and reduce risk of harm to children (child protection/safeguarding). The other options are unsafe or fail to protect vulnerable children.
156. A student formulates the following: “To prove that poor staffing causes medication errors among nurses.” An appropriate critique for this objective is;
A. The objective lacks measurable variables for data collection
B. The objective contains insufficient details about study participants
C. The objective focuses excessively on descriptive statistical methods
D. The objective uses a biased action verb suggesting predetermined findings
Answer: D. The objective uses a biased action verb suggesting predetermined findings
Rationale: The verb 'to prove' presupposes the outcome, introducing researcher bias; sound research objectives seek to investigate, examine or determine relationships without a predetermined conclusion (Polit & Beck; standard research methodology). This is the primary methodological flaw.
157. A community health nurse collects data on mothers’ perceptions regarding childhood immunization in a remote village. The data collected are mainly narratives, beliefs, and experiences. The most appropriate type of research would be;
A. Qualitative research exploring meanings and lived experiences
B. Quantitative research using numerical statistical comparisons
C. Correlational research measuring relationships among variables
D. Experimental research involving random assignment procedures
Answer: A. Qualitative research exploring meanings and lived experiences
Rationale: Narratives, beliefs and experiences are non-numerical data best captured by qualitative research, which explores meanings and lived experiences of participants (Polit & Beck; NCK research syllabus). Numerical/correlational/experimental designs do not fit narrative data.
158. A nursing researcher investigating patient satisfaction uses age and educational level as variables that cannot be manipulated. These variables are best classified as:
A. Extraneous variables threatening internal validity of findings
B. Active variables created by the investigator during intervention
C. Demographic or attribute variables beyond researcher control
D. Continuous variables measured along an infinite numerical scale
Answer: C. Demographic or attribute variables beyond researcher control
Rationale: Age and educational level are pre-existing characteristics of participants that cannot be manipulated; these are demographic/attribute variables (Polit & Beck). Active variables are created by the investigator, which these are not.
159. The following is true about the nominal scale of measurement; -
A. OptionCategories are ranked in an increasing order to represent classes
B. It has a true zero point with meaningful mathematical operations
C. It is the best scale for test scores representing the sample attributes
D. Numerals assigned to various categories do not represent quantities
Answer: D. Numerals assigned to various categories do not represent quantities
Rationale: The nominal scale simply classifies data into mutually exclusive categories (e.g. sex, blood group); assigned numerals are labels only and carry no quantitative or rank value (standard biostatistics). It has no order, no true zero and no arithmetic meaning.
160. A facilitator notices that adult learners disengage whenever lengthy theoretical explanations are used without practical relevance. Which principle of andragogy is most applicable in this case;
A. Adults learn best through externally directed instruction
B. Adults learn effectively through repetitive memorization
C. Adults learn adequately through standardized uniform experiences
D. Adults learn optimally when content solves immediate problems
Answer: D. Adults learn optimally when content solves immediate problems
Rationale: Knowles' andragogy holds that adults are problem-centred and motivated when learning has immediate, practical relevance to real problems (orientation to learning). Disengagement with abstract theory reflects a need for problem-solving relevance, not externally directed rote learning.
161. A patient with obsessive compulsive personality disorder;
A. Is never comfortable unless he/she is the centre of attention.
B. Almost always chooses solitary places and activities.
C. Avoids intimate relationship for fear of being ashamed.
D. Is excessively devoted to work at the expense of other things.
Answer: D. Is excessively devoted to work at the expense of other things.
Rationale: Obsessive-compulsive personality disorder is characterised by preoccupation with orderliness, perfectionism and control, including excessive devotion to work and productivity to the exclusion of leisure and relationships (DSM-5; Kenya mental health nursing). The other options describe histrionic, schizoid and avoidant traits.
162. Theoretical framework refers to;
A. Gives contrasting variables in a study
B. Diagrammatic representation of how variables are related
C. A previous described model that a researcher applies to their study
D. Representations of how problem statement is linked to the literature
Answer: C. A previous described model that a researcher applies to their study
Rationale: A theoretical framework is an existing, previously described theory or model the researcher adopts and applies to guide and interpret the study (Polit & Beck). It differs from a conceptual framework, which diagrammatically shows how the researcher's own variables relate.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
163. During a pediatric rotation, a student nurse observes that hospitalized children separated from parents frequently appear anxious and withdrawn. She concludes that separation from parents increases stress among children. Which reasoning process is illustrated?
A. Deductive reasoning from theory toward specific observations
B. Experimental reasoning using intervention and comparison groups
C. Statistical reasoning based on numerical probability analysis
D. Inductive reasoning from observations toward general conclusions
Answer: D. Inductive reasoning from observations toward general conclusions
Rationale: Moving from specific observations (individual anxious children) to a general conclusion (separation increases stress) is inductive reasoning (Polit & Beck). Deductive reasoning moves from general theory to specific cases, the reverse direction.
164. A student consistently demonstrates empathy, cooperation, and ethical conduct during patient interactions. The affective domain level best represented is;
A. Responding through active participation in events
B. Valuing through expression of positive attitudes
C. Organization through balancing multiple beliefs
D. Portrayal through consistent value-driven behavior
Answer: D. Portrayal through consistent value-driven behavior
Rationale: Consistent empathy, cooperation and ethical conduct reflect the highest affective domain level (characterization by value / value complex), where internalised values consistently drive behaviour (Krathwohl's affective taxonomy). This exceeds mere responding or valuing.
165. A tutorial format that ensures every participant becomes progressively involved in group discussion is;
A. Syndicate discussion involving divided assignments
B. Panel discussion involving selected presenters
C. Snowball discussion involving pyramidal participation
D. Small group discussion involving opposing viewpoints
Answer: C. Snowball discussion involving pyramidal participation
Rationale: The snowball (pyramid) technique progressively builds participation from pairs to fours to larger groups, ensuring every participant becomes involved incrementally (educational methods; NCK teaching methodology). Syndicate and panel formats limit involvement to subgroups or selected presenters.
166. A facilitator intentionally redirects students’ questions back to the discussion group instead of immediately providing answers. The teaching and learning theory being reflected in this case is;
A. Thorndike theory emphasizing stimulus-response linkage
B. Behaviorist theory emphasizing reinforcement approaches
C. Humanistic theory emphasizing learner-centered discovery
D. Classical conditioning emphasizing stimulus association
Answer: C. Humanistic theory emphasizing learner-centered discovery
Rationale: Redirecting questions back to the group so students construct answers themselves reflects humanistic, learner-centred, discovery learning where the facilitator guides rather than dispenses knowledge. This is distinct from behaviourist/stimulus-response approaches. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals and NCK nursing education/teaching-learning theory principles.
167. The scientific period of mental health nursing has been majorly characterized by;
A. Training schools for mental health care providers.
B. The belief that the mentally ill individuals are cursed.
C. Advocating for the release of chained mentally ill patients.
D. Passing of laws to protect the properties of the mentally ill.
Answer: A. Training schools for mental health care providers.
Rationale: The scientific (modern) period of mental health nursing is characterised by professionalisation, including the establishment of formal training schools for mental health care providers and evidence-based care. Beliefs in curses/superstition belong to the primitive era, and Pinel's release of chained patients belongs to the moral-treatment reform era. Reference: NCK Mental Health/Psychiatric Nursing curriculum.
168. During psychiatric formulation the nurse will record the following information in Part-I;
A. Clinical manifestations of the illness.
B. Causes and predisposing factors to the illness.
C. Previous episodes of the illness if available.
D. Possible diagnosis and differential diagnosis.
Answer: A. Clinical manifestations of the illness.
Rationale: In a psychiatric formulation, Part I is the descriptive/summary section documenting the clinical picture (presenting features and manifestations) of the illness. Aetiology/predisposing factors, differential diagnosis and prognosis are recorded in later parts of the formulation. Reference: NCK Mental Health Nursing psychiatric formulation guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
169. A researcher studying ICU nurses first divides participants into day-shift and night-shift groups, then randomly selects participants from each group. The sampling method illustrated is;
A. Cluster sampling involving naturally occurring institutional units
B. Convenience sampling involving easily accessible participants
C. Stratified random sampling using subgroup representation
D. Purposive sampling using participant referral recruitment methods
Answer: C. Stratified random sampling using subgroup representation
Rationale: Dividing participants into distinct subgroups (day-shift and night-shift strata) and then randomly selecting from each stratum is the defining procedure of stratified random sampling, ensuring representation of each subgroup. Reference: NCK Research & Statistics curriculum.
170. A researcher intentionally recruits participants from all social classes without discrimination during a community health study. The ethical principle illustrated in this case is;
A. Beneficence through maximizing study-related patient benefits
B. Nonmaleficence through prevention of physical discomfort
C. Justice through fair and equitable participant selection
D. Confidentiality involving secure management of study records
Answer: C. Justice through fair and equitable participant selection
Rationale: Recruiting participants fairly from all social classes without discrimination reflects the ethical principle of justice, which requires equitable selection and fair distribution of research burdens and benefits. Reference: NCK research ethics; Belmont principles.
171. The following action directly enhances meaningful learning according to the principles of learning;
A. Continuous use of teacher-centered lectures
B. Avoidance of learner participation activities
C. Presentation of unrelated advanced concepts
D. Sequential and logical organization of material
Answer: D. Sequential and logical organization of material
Rationale: Meaningful learning is enhanced when content is arranged sequentially and logically so new information links to prior knowledge (organisation principle). Teacher-centred lectures, avoiding participation, and unrelated advanced concepts all impair meaningful learning. Reference: Kozier Fundamentals; NCK principles of teaching and learning.
172. It is generally considered that cognitive behavioral therapy changes;
A. Thought and learning process.
B. Behaviour and affect.
C. Thought and behaviour.
D. Mood and hypnotic states
Answer: C. Thought and behaviour.
Rationale: Cognitive behavioural therapy targets and modifies maladaptive thoughts (cognitions) and behaviours, as its name indicates, to improve functioning and mood. Reference: NCK Mental Health Nursing curriculum; standard psychiatric texts.
173. The best timing for effective reinforcement in teaching is;
A. Immediate and meaningful learner response
B. Delayed and highly technical learner feedback
C. General and emotionally neutral communication
D. Complex and highly individualized correction
Answer: A. Immediate and meaningful learner response
Rationale: Reinforcement is most effective when given immediately and is meaningful to the learner, strengthening the desired response (law of effect/reinforcement timing). Delayed, neutral, or overly complex feedback weakens the reinforcement effect. Reference: NCK principles of teaching and learning; Kozier Fundamentals.
174. A teacher praises learners immediately after accurate responses during classroom discussion. According to behaviorist theory. The purpose of this action is to;
A. Reduce dependence on environmental influences
B. Encourage independent conceptual restructuring
C. Increase recurrence of desirable learner behavior
D. Promote abstract reasoning through reflection
Answer: C. Increase recurrence of desirable learner behavior
Rationale: Praise following a correct response is positive reinforcement in behaviorist (Skinner) theory, which strengthens and increases the likelihood that the desirable behavior recurs. Reflection and conceptual restructuring belong to cognitive/constructivist theories, not behaviorism.
175. A nursing examination accurately measures students’ knowledge of infection prevention rather than unrelated mathematical skills. This demonstrates what type of validity in research;
A. Face validity based on appearance of relevance
B. Construct validity measuring the intended concept accurately
C. External validity involving generalization to wider populations
D. Predictive validity estimating future clinical performance outcomes
Answer: B. Construct validity measuring the intended concept accurately
Rationale: An exam that measures the intended construct (infection prevention knowledge) and not an irrelevant construct (mathematics) demonstrates construct validity, the degree to which a tool measures the theoretical concept it claims to measure. Face validity is only about surface appearance; external and predictive validity concern generalization and future outcomes.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
176. The defense mechanism commonly employed by depressed patients is;
A. Rationalization.
B. Intellectualization.
C. Displacement.
D. Introjection.
Answer: D. Introjection.
Rationale: In depression the characteristic defense mechanism is introjection, whereby feelings (often anger toward a lost object) are turned inward against the self, producing guilt and self-devaluation. This is the standard psychiatric nursing explanation for the dynamics of depression.
177. A researcher conducts a literature review before designing a study on adolescent pregnancy. What major purpose is best achieved through the literature review in research;
A. Determining the exact statistical tests for final analysis
B. Ensuring all study participants receive equal interventions
C. Identifying gaps and inconsistencies in existing evidence
D. Establishing randomization procedures for participant allocation
Answer: C. Identifying gaps and inconsistencies in existing evidence
Rationale: A literature review situates the study within existing knowledge and its main purpose is to identify gaps, inconsistencies and unresolved questions that justify the new study. Statistical test selection, randomization and intervention delivery are methodological/design steps, not the purpose of the review.
178. A community psychiatric nurse visits a stable schizophrenic patient at home to assess medication adherence and family coping. This approach best reflects:
A. Forensic psychiatry focusing on legal mental health evaluation
B. Biological psychiatry emphasizing pharmacological symptom suppression
C. Institutional psychiatry emphasizing prolonged inpatient management care
D. Community psychiatry promoting continuity within natural environments
Answer: D. Community psychiatry promoting continuity within natural environments
Rationale: Home visiting of a stable patient to support medication adherence and family coping exemplifies community psychiatry, which delivers continuity of care within the patient's natural home and social environment. It contrasts with institutional (inpatient) and forensic (legal) approaches.
179. Systematic over-representation of some segments of the population in terms of the characteristics relevant to the research question is; -
A. Sampling bias
B. Sampling error
C. Sampling fluctuation
D. Sampling distribution
Answer: A. Sampling bias
Rationale: Systematic over-representation of certain population segments on characteristics relevant to the research question is sampling bias, a systematic (non-random) distortion of the sample. Sampling error is random chance variation, and sampling distribution is a theoretical distribution of a statistic.
180. The two key qualities of a variable are that they should be:
A. Broad and measurable
B. Abstract and broad
C. Abstract and measurable
D. Changing and measurable
Answer: D. Changing and measurable
Rationale: A variable, by definition, is a characteristic that varies (changes/takes different values) and must be measurable/observable so it can be studied. Abstract concepts must be operationalized to become variables, so 'abstract' is not a defining quality.
181. In Problem Based Learning, which step occurs immediately after formulation of learning objectives in the seven-step tutorial process;
A. Synthesis of newly acquired group information
B. Clarification of unfamiliar concepts and terms
C. Student centred gathering of additional information
D. Development of systematic explanatory inventory
Answer: C. Student centred gathering of additional information
Rationale: In the Maastricht seven-step (seven-jump) PBL process, after formulating the learning objectives (step 5) students undertake independent, self-directed study to gather additional information (step 6), followed by synthesis/reporting back (step 7). Clarifying terms is step 1, so it precedes rather than follows objective setting.
182. Regarding snowball sampling method;
A. The population is selected according to the defined subgroups
B. Use of a table of random numbers is considered in selection of the sample
C. Allows participants already in the study to provide referrals to potential study subjects
D. Participants are selected because they possess the required characteristics or are knowledgeable about the issues under investigations.
Answer: C. Allows participants already in the study to provide referrals to potential study subjects
Rationale: Snowball (chain-referral) sampling is a non-probability technique in which existing participants recruit or refer future subjects from their networks, useful for hard-to-reach or hidden populations. Option A describes stratified sampling, B random sampling, and D purposive sampling (Polit & Beck; NCK research methods).
183. A hospital-based study cannot recruit enough eligible participants despite extensive planning. The category of research challenge presented is;
A. Ethical challenge related to participant rights violations
B. Conceptual challenge involving unclear theoretical frameworks
C. Practical challenge involving participant recruitment difficulties
D. Administrative challenge involving poor task management systems
Answer: C. Practical challenge involving participant recruitment difficulties
Rationale: Inability to recruit enough eligible participants despite good planning is a practical/logistical challenge in the conduct of research, not an ethical, conceptual, or administrative one. It relates to feasibility of the study (NCK research methods; Polit & Beck).
184. A type of curriculum that emphasizes mastery of separate academic modules is;
A. Subject-centered curriculum promoting content specialization
B. Experience-centered curriculum promoting practical exposure
C. Learner-centered curriculum promoting individualized inquiry
D. Integrated curriculum promoting interdisciplinary learning
Answer: A. Subject-centered curriculum promoting content specialization
Rationale: A subject-centered curriculum organizes learning around separate academic subjects/modules with emphasis on mastery of discrete content, unlike integrated, learner-centered or experience-centered approaches. This is standard curriculum theory in nursing education (NCK curriculum development).
185. An appropriate teaching method to use when introducing a completely new topic to a large audience within limited time is;
A. Role play involving learner interaction
B. Syndicate discussion involving subgroup reporting
C. Lecture involving organized oral presentation
D. Tutorial involving intensive small-group dialogue
Answer: C. Lecture involving organized oral presentation
Rationale: The lecture method is best suited for delivering new information to a large audience within limited time through an organized oral presentation. Role play, syndicate and tutorial methods are learner-intensive and impractical for large groups in short time (NCK teaching methods).
186. A tutor consistently redirects students to identify solutions independently before offering guidance. The “super skill” of innovative education that is highly reflected in this case is;
A. Participate through direct problem solving
B. Inform through structured factual explanation
C. Guide through facilitative learner support
D. Withdraw through minimal tutorial interaction
Answer: C. Guide through facilitative learner support
Rationale: A tutor who redirects students to find solutions themselves before offering help demonstrates the facilitative 'guide' super skill of innovative/facilitative education, promoting self-directed learning rather than direct instruction or withdrawal (NCK innovative teaching/facilitation).
187. A teacher structures content from known concepts toward unfamiliar concepts to improve comprehension of new material. A theorist who strongly supports this approach is;
A. D. P. Ausubel through meaningful prior knowledge integration
B. Abraham Maslow through self-enhancement motivation
C. F. Skinner through operant reinforcement sequencing
D. Ivan Pavlov through conditioned stimulus pairing
Answer: A. D. P. Ausubel through meaningful prior knowledge integration
Rationale: Ausubel's theory of meaningful learning stresses building new material upon relevant prior knowledge (advance organizers), moving from the known to the unknown. This matches the described teaching sequence per NCK nursing education foundations.
188. During a clinical teaching session, students perform a procedure accurately, efficiently, and with minimal conscious effort. The psychomotor stage that is most likely to be achieved is;
A. Manipulation involving guided coordinated movements
B. Precision involving proficient supervised performance
C. Articulation involving coordinated communication patterns
D. Naturalization involving automatic integrated performance
Answer: D. Naturalization involving automatic integrated performance
Rationale: In Simpson's psychomotor domain, naturalization is the highest level where performance becomes automatic, effortless and integrated. Accurate, efficient performance with minimal conscious effort defines this stage per nursing education taxonomy.
189. A nurse researcher develops a study to test whether a structured patient education program reduces hospital readmission among diabetic patients. Participants are randomly assigned into intervention and control groups. Features that make this a true experimental design are;
A. Inclusion of manipulation, control, and randomization processes
B. Use of multiple hospitals to improve participant recruitment
C. Use of questionnaires to obtain patient satisfaction scores
D. Observation of participants over several months of follow-up
Answer: A. Inclusion of manipulation, control, and randomization processes
Rationale: A true (classic) experimental design requires manipulation of the independent variable, a control group, and random assignment of participants. These three defining features are described in the study per nursing research texts.
190. In communicable diseases, the route of transmission can be interrupted by:-
A. Providing treatment to all the sick people
B. Vaccinating the population
C. Attacking the vectors
D. Notification
Answer: C. Attacking the vectors
Rationale: Interrupting the route/mode of transmission targets how the agent travels from source to host; vector control (e.g., killing mosquitoes/insects) directly breaks the transmission link. Treating the sick attacks the reservoir, and vaccination protects the host per Kenya communicable disease control principles.
191. Water- washed infections can be prevented through
A. Improving the quality of water
B. Reducing contact with contaminated water
C. Notification
Answer: B. Reducing contact with contaminated water
Rationale: Water-washed diseases arise from insufficient water for hygiene and are prevented by improving water quantity/hygiene and reducing contact, not by improving water quality (that prevents water-borne disease). Reducing contact with contaminated water is the best listed option per Kenya environmental health guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
192. In home visiting, the community health nurse shares information on purpose of the visit, during the:-
A. Initiation phase
B. Home visiting phase
C. Pre-visit activities
D. Post-visit activities
Answer: A. Initiation phase
Rationale: During the initiation/introductory phase of a home visit the community health nurse greets the family, establishes rapport, and states the purpose of the visit. This precedes the working (home visiting) phase per community health nursing practice.
193. The cardinal sign of stage 3 of cholera is:-
A. Improvement of general condition
B. Rice-water appearance of stool
C. Shock
D. Severe abdominal cramps
Answer: C. Shock
Rationale: Cholera progresses through stages; stage 3 (algid/collapse stage) is characterised by profound dehydration leading to hypovolaemic shock. Rice-water stool is the early presenting sign, whereas shock marks the advanced stage per Kenya cholera management guidelines.
194. In community diagnosis, developing objectives as to why the survey is being carried out is done during:-
A. Exploration stage
B. Pre-testing of instruments
C. Execution  stage
D. Planning stage
Answer: D. Planning stage
Rationale: In community diagnosis, formulating the objectives and rationale for the survey is part of the planning stage, which precedes instrument pre-testing and data collection (execution). This follows the community diagnosis process in community health nursing.
195. The process by which subclinical cases of disease are identified is known as:-
A. Contact tracing
B. Screening
C. Surveillance
D. Clinical diagnosis
Answer: B. Screening
Rationale: Screening is the presumptive identification of unrecognized or subclinical disease in apparently healthy persons using rapid tests or examinations. This is standard epidemiology per Kenya MOH and community health texts. Surveillance monitors disease trends, and diagnosis confirms clinical cases.
196. In the investigation of an epidemic, the most appropriate measure to describe the frequency of           occurrence of illness is the:
A. Prevalence rate
B. Incidence rate
C. Case-fatality rate
D. Attack rate
Answer: D. Attack rate
Rationale: In an epidemic/outbreak investigation, the attack rate (a form of incidence measuring new cases among the exposed population over a limited period) is the most appropriate measure of frequency of illness. Prevalence and incidence rates apply to endemic/ongoing measurement, per standard epidemiology.
197. The dietary sources for vitamin B12 include:-
A. Legumes and citrus fruits
B. Liver and green vegetables
C. Fish and eggs
D. Milk and green vegetables
Answer: C. Fish and eggs
Rationale: Vitamin B12 (cobalamin) is found almost exclusively in animal-source foods such as fish, eggs, meat, liver and dairy. Among the options, fish and eggs are both rich animal sources; green vegetables/legumes/citrus are not reliable B12 sources. Per Kenya nutrition guidelines and Kozier.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
198. For cold chain maintenance, the temperatures should range between
A. 0oC  to +8oC
B. +2 oC to +8 oC
C. +4 oC to +8 oC
D. 0 oC to +10 oC1.
Answer: B. +2 oC to +8 oC
Rationale: Per Kenya EPI/cold chain and WHO guidelines, vaccines at the health facility refrigerator level must be stored between +2°C and +8°C. This is the standard cold chain range for the lowest storage levels.
199. The following is a method of secondary prevention of tuberculosis:-
A. Immunization with BCG.
B. Prophylactic treatment of people at risk.
C. Treating patients suffering from tuberculosis.
D. Improving the nutritional status of the community.
Answer: C. Treating patients suffering from tuberculosis.
Rationale: Secondary prevention involves early detection and prompt treatment to halt disease progression and cut transmission; treating TB patients is secondary prevention. BCG immunization and improving nutrition are primary prevention. Per Kenya TB programme and community health.
200. The pillars of primary health care include:-
A. Use of scientifically sound and socially acceptable methods, addressing common health problems, supply of essential drugs.
B. Universal accessibility by individuals and families, appropriate health infrastructure, water supply and sanitation.
C. Sustainability, community participation, supply of essential drugs.
D. Affordability, community participation, supply of essential drugs.
Answer: B. Universal accessibility by individuals and families, appropriate health infrastructure, water supply and sanitation.
Rationale: The pillars of PHC include universal accessibility/coverage, community participation, intersectoral collaboration and appropriate technology. Option B best captures accessibility and supportive infrastructure/sanitation. Per Kenya community health and PHC (Alma-Ata) framework.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
201. Some of the advantages of home visiting are that the nurse:-
A. Assesses the health status of all family members and clients feel free to communicate because of being in a familiar environment.
B. Carries out required investigations and assesses the health status of family members.
C. Carries out demonstrations and assesses immunization coverage within the community.
D. Carries out treatment of minor ailments and administers mass vaccinations.
Answer: A. Assesses the health status of all family members and clients feel free to communicate because of being in a familiar environment.
Rationale: Home visiting allows the nurse to assess the whole family in their natural setting, and clients communicate more freely in a familiar environment. Mass vaccination and major investigations are not primary advantages of home visits. Per Kenya community health nursing practice.
202. A community may be defined as:-
A. Individuals and groups living and interacting within certain boundaries.
B. Groups of people sharing common health problems.
C. Groups sharing same boundaries.
D. Families sharing common cultural beliefs.
Answer: A. Individuals and groups living and interacting within certain boundaries.
Rationale: A community is a group of individuals and families living and interacting within defined geographical or social boundaries, sharing common interests, values or institutions. Per community health nursing definitions.
203. The following are some of the functions of a school health nurse:-
A. Physical assessment of pupils and staff, health education, public relations.
B. Planning and conducting health care activities, improving the school environment, basic training of teachers.
C. First Aid and emergency care, teaching first aid and nutrition, counseling.
D. Basic training of teachers, health education, immunization and counseling.
Answer: A. Physical assessment of pupils and staff, health education, public relations.
Rationale: The school health nurse's core functions include physical/health assessment of pupils (and staff), health education, and maintaining public relations/liaison with the school and community. Kenya School Health Policy and community health nursing texts list these as legitimate SHN roles. The other options mix in tasks like 'basic training of teachers' that are not core SHN functions, making option A the most accurate composite.
204. The functions of roughage include:-
A. Promoting movement of food through the digestive system, aiding elimination of wastes from the digestive system, preventing constipation.
B. Providing vitamins, aiding in defecation, helping in regulating body temperature.
C. Satisfying hunger, preventing prolapse of rectum, regulating meatabolic rate.
D. Regulating the functions of muscles and nerves, aiding in defaecation, regulating metabolic rate.
Answer: A. Promoting movement of food through the digestive system, aiding elimination of wastes from the digestive system, preventing constipation.
Rationale: Dietary fibre (roughage) adds bulk, stimulates peristalsis to move food along the gut, aids elimination of waste, and prevents constipation. Kozier Fundamentals and nutrition texts describe exactly these functions. Fibre itself does not provide vitamins or regulate body temperature, eliminating the other options.
205. Some of the requirements for a good rural house are:-
A. Adequate water supply, made of a hard floor and walls, has a pit latrine.
B. Should be built on dry ground, separate accommodation for humans and animals has a good pit latrine.
C. Separate provisions for food storage and prevention, adequate light and ventilation, piped water supply.
D. Adequate means of refuse disposal, built away from flooding, separate bedroom for each family member.
Answer: B. Should be built on dry ground, separate accommodation for humans and animals has a good pit latrine.
Rationale: Environmental health standards for a good rural house require siting on dry, well-drained ground, separation of human and animal housing, and safe excreta disposal (a good pit latrine). Piped water is not realistic as a rural-house requirement, and 'separate bedroom for each family member' is not a standard rural requirement, so option B is the best composite per Kenya environmental health guidance.
206. Cold chain is defined as a system of:-
A. Immunizing children and expectant mothers with a potent vaccine.
B. Maintaining vaccines in a potent state from the manufacturer to the consumer.
C. Maintaining the vaccine in a potent state in a health facility.
D. Transporting vaccines from the manufacturer to the consumer.
Answer: B. Maintaining vaccines in a potent state from the manufacturer to the consumer.
Rationale: The cold chain is the system of maintaining vaccines within the correct temperature range (potent state) at every stage from the manufacturer through transport and storage to the point of use (the child/consumer). Kenya EPI/KEPI guidelines define it as end-to-end, which distinguishes B from options limited to only transport or only the facility.
207. The performance of an immunization programme can be evaluated through:-
A. Supervisory visits, special surveys, BCG scars.
B. Routine reporting, mass campaigns, special surveys.
C. Routine supervisory visits, special surveys, routine reporting.
D. Cold chain maintenance, special surveys, supervisory visits.
Answer: C. Routine supervisory visits, special surveys, routine reporting.
Rationale: Immunization programme performance is evaluated through routine reporting (coverage/administrative data), routine supervisory visits, and special surveys (e.g., coverage surveys). Mass campaigns and cold chain maintenance are activities/inputs, not evaluation methods, so option C best captures the standard EPI monitoring and evaluation methods.
208. The measures taken to prevent dental carries include:-
A. Limiting sugary foods, regular dental check-up, maintenance of good oral hygiene.
B. Regular brushing of teeth, limiting sugary foods, avoiding smoking.
C. Balanced diet, physical exercises, proper oral hygiene.
D. Exercising the teeth by chewing regularly, dental check up, high carbohydrate diet.
Answer: A. Limiting sugary foods, regular dental check-up, maintenance of good oral hygiene.
Rationale: Prevention of dental caries centres on reducing dietary sugars, regular dental check-ups for early detection, and good oral hygiene (brushing, and where available fluoride). Option A lists these evidence-based measures, whereas other options include irrelevant or harmful items such as 'high carbohydrate diet'.
209. In community diagnosis, demographic data includes:-
A. Population size, population density, population composition
B. Population composition, cultural patterns, occupation.
C. Population size, population density, occupation.
D. Population density, cultural patterns, population size.
Answer: A. Population size, population density, population composition
Rationale: Demographic data in community diagnosis describe the population's characteristics: size, density, and composition (age, sex, distribution). Occupation and cultural patterns are socio-economic/cultural data, not demographic, so option A is the correct set per community health nursing texts.
210. A six year old child presents at the child clinic with oedema and his weight is 60% below normal. This child is likely to be suffering from:
A. Underweight
B. Kwashiorkor
C. Marasmus
D. Marasmic kwashiorkor
Answer: B. Kwashiorkor
Rationale: Bilateral pitting oedema is the defining clinical hallmark of kwashiorkor (oedematous protein-energy malnutrition), and Kenya IMNCI/nutrition protocols classify oedema of both feet as oedematous severe acute malnutrition. Marasmus is severe wasting WITHOUT oedema, so the presence of oedema points to kwashiorkor. Marasmic kwashiorkor requires clearly documented severe visible wasting together with oedema; the stem emphasises oedema as the presenting feature, making kwashiorkor the intended single best answer.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
211. A disease with a constant high prevalence in a population is referred to as:
A. Epidemic
B. Hyperendemic
C. Endemic
D. Pandemic
Answer: C. Endemic
Rationale: A disease with a constant, habitual presence at a relatively stable prevalence within a given population/geographic area is termed endemic. Hyperendemic implies persistently high and equal transmission, while epidemic and pandemic denote excess/spreading occurrence. Per Kenya community health and standard epidemiology texts.
212. The Lactational amenorrhoea method is effective up to:
A. 6 weeks
B. 4 months
C. 6 months
D. 3 months
Answer: C. 6 months
Rationale: The Lactational Amenorrhoea Method (LAM) is effective for up to 6 months postpartum, provided the mother is fully/nearly fully breastfeeding and remains amenorrhoeic. Per Kenya National Family Planning Guidelines.
213. The following are water washed diseases
A. Trachoma, poliomyelitis
B. Malaria, amoebiasis
C. Trachoma, scabies
D. Hepatitis A, Typhoid
Answer: C. Trachoma, scabies
Rationale: Water-washed diseases result from insufficient water for hygiene rather than contaminated water; classic examples are trachoma and scabies (also other skin/eye infections). Water-borne diseases like typhoid, hepatitis A and amoebiasis are contaminated-water diseases. Per Kenya environmental health references.
214. The following is a level three health facility according to Kenya national health system.
A. District hospital
B. Health centre
C. Dispensary
D. Provincial hospital
Answer: B. Health centre
Rationale: In the Kenya health system tiers (KEPH), Level 1 = community, Level 2 = dispensaries, Level 3 = health centres, Level 4 = primary/sub-county hospitals, Levels 5-6 = county referral and national referral hospitals. A health centre is therefore a Level 3 facility.
215. The main focus of the Alma Ata declaration was
A. Reduction of maternal mortality
B. Eradication of poverty
C. Eradication of communicable and immunizable diseases
D. Essential health care
Answer: D. Essential health care
Rationale: The 1978 Alma Ata Declaration launched Primary Health Care, defining essential health care made universally accessible as the key to attaining Health for All. Its central focus was essential (primary) health care. Per WHO/Kenya community health references.
216. In community diagnosis process, one should gather background information in which stage;
A. Exploration
B. Execution of the study
C. Data handling
D. Pilot study
Answer: A. Exploration
Rationale: In the community diagnosis process, gathering of background/existing information about the community occurs during the exploration (preparatory) stage, before designing and executing the study. Per Kenya community health nursing references.
217. The level of the mind that acts in a  socially appropriate manner is
A. Id
B. Ego
C. Super Ego
D. Unconscious mind
Answer: B. Ego
Rationale: In Freud's structural model, the ego operates on the reality principle, mediating between id and superego to behave in a socially appropriate, reality-based manner. The superego is the moral/conscience component and the id seeks immediate gratification. Per mental health nursing references.
218. A DPHN involved the ministry of agriculture in improving the nutrition of the district. This principle of PHC is referred to as:
A. Community participation
B. Appropriate technology
C. Inter-sectoral collaboration
D. Nutrition and adequate food supply
Answer: C. Inter-sectoral collaboration
Rationale: Engaging another sector such as the Ministry of Agriculture to improve nutrition exemplifies inter-sectoral collaboration, a core PHC principle recognizing that health is influenced by sectors beyond the health system. Per Alma Ata/PHC principles and Kenya community health references.
219. The correct advice to the community on proper chlorination of drinking water is;
A. Use 2% chlorine  and give a minimum contact period of 15 minutes
B. Use 2% chlorine  and give a minimum contact period of 30 minutes
C. Use  1% chlorine  and give a minimum contact period of 15 minutes
D. Use 1%  chlorine  and give a minimum contact period of 30 minutes
Answer: D. Use 1%  chlorine  and give a minimum contact period of 30 minutes
Rationale: Standard water chlorination for community drinking water requires a minimum contact time of 30 minutes to achieve adequate disinfection, using a 1% chlorine solution. Per Kenya environmental/public health water treatment guidelines.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
220. The following vaccines can be damaged by sunlight;
A. BCG and Polio
B. Pneumococcal and Measles
C. Polio and yellow fever
D. BCG and measles
Answer: D. BCG and measles
Rationale: BCG and measles vaccines are live attenuated freeze-dried vaccines that are sensitive to and readily destroyed by heat and light (sunlight/UV). Kenya EPI cold-chain guidelines require these to be protected from light and reconstituted only when needed and discarded after 6 hours. Polio (OPV) is heat-sensitive but the classic light-sensitive pair examined is BCG and measles.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
221. Pit latrines should be at least;
A. 2m above the water table
B. 3m away from the house.
C. 6m away from water source.
D. 6m deep
Answer: A. 2m above the water table
Rationale: Kenya environmental health/sanitation standards require the pit latrine base to remain at least about 2 metres above the water table to prevent groundwater contamination, and be sited at least 30m from a water source. Of the options given, 2m above the water table is the correct minimum standard.
222. Community diagnosis results can be presented by use of the following.
A. Frequency polygon, table of random numbers, pie charts
B. Histogram, frequency tables, pie charts
C. Tally sheets, frequency polygon, table of random numbers
D. Tally sheets, pie charts, histograms.
Answer: D. Tally sheets, pie charts, histograms.
Rationale: Community diagnosis data are presented and displayed using tally sheets (for recording counts) and graphical tools such as pie charts and histograms. A table of random numbers is a sampling tool, not a data-presentation method, so options containing it are wrong.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
223. At a certain health centre the total number of new cases of tuberculosis recorded in the month of April was 50. This is an example of;
A. Incidence
B. Prevalence
C. Morbidity
D. Epidemic
Answer: A. Incidence
Rationale: Incidence is the number of NEW cases of a disease occurring in a defined population during a specified time period. Fifty new TB cases in April is a count of new cases over a set time, i.e. incidence, distinguishing it from prevalence (all existing cases).
224. The following statement  best describes how resources should be distributed according to PHC principles;
A. Equally among all community members with needs.
B. As perceived by the community leaders.
C. Fairly among all individuals and families.
D. As perceived by donor community.
Answer: C. Fairly among all individuals and families.
Rationale: A core PHC principle is equity, meaning resources are distributed fairly among all individuals and families according to need. This reflects the Alma-Ata/PHC principle of equitable distribution rather than distribution based on leaders' or donors' perceptions.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
225. Intrauterine contraceptive devices;
A. Should be inserted only during menstruation.
B. Should not be inserted before 6 weeks post partum.
C. Can migrate to other organs like the heart
D. Can be used by a client with ischemic heart disease
Answer: C. Can migrate to other organs like the heart
Rationale: A rare but recognised complication of IUCD use is uterine perforation with migration of the device into the abdominal cavity or adjacent organs. IUCDs can be inserted at any time in the cycle if pregnancy is excluded and immediately postpartum, and are actually suitable for women with ischaemic heart disease as they are non-hormonal; thus migration is the correct true statement.
226. During community survey one of the subjects refuses to be interviewed. The best action to take is;
A. Proceed to the next subject without uttering a word
B. Tell the respondent that you will come back later
C. Persuade the subject to cooperative
D. Thank the interviewee for his time and proceed to next home.
Answer: D. Thank the interviewee for his time and proceed to next home.
Rationale: Participation in a community survey is voluntary and informed consent must be respected. When a subject refuses, the ethical and professional action is to thank them for their time and move on to the next household, respecting autonomy rather than coercing or persuading them.
227. The central government of Kenya started running health service in;
A. 1972
B. 1964
C. 1963
D. 1970
Answer: C. 1963
Rationale: Kenya attained independence in 1963, and the central government took over the running of national health services at that point. This is the standard reference date in Kenyan health systems history.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
228. The acronym SWOT refers to;
A. Specific, worthiness, opportunistic, time bound
B. Specific, weakness, opportunities, time bound
C. Strength, weakness, opportunities and threats
D. Strength, worthiness, opportunities, threats
Answer: C. Strength, weakness, opportunities and threats
Rationale: SWOT is a management/planning analysis tool standing for Strengths, Weaknesses, Opportunities and Threats. This is the standard definition used in nursing management and health planning.
229. The purpose of vaccine forecasting is;
A. Ordering vaccine to avoid stock outs.
B. Determining the population that require vaccination
C. Estimating the number of persons immunized
D. Maintenance of potency of vaccines.
Answer: A. Ordering vaccine to avoid stock outs.
Rationale: Vaccine forecasting estimates future vaccine needs so that adequate quantities are ordered and supplied, preventing stock-outs and wastage. Per Kenya EPI logistics guidelines, forecasting underpins procurement and supply planning to ensure continuous vaccine availability.
230. The best method of preventing water washed diseases is;
A. Improve the quality of water
B. Minimize the  contact between people and water
C. Protect water sources from contamination
D. Improve the quantity of water.
Answer: D. Improve the quantity of water.
Rationale: Water-washed (water-scarce) diseases such as scabies, trachoma, and skin/eye infections result from insufficient water for personal hygiene, so the key preventive measure is increasing the QUANTITY of water available for washing and hygiene (Kenya Environmental Health/PHC principles). Improving water quality prevents water-borne diseases, not water-washed diseases. The correct answer is to improve the quantity of water.
231. The immediate care of a patient confirmed to have cholera at the health centre is;
A. Administer intravenous fluid, refer the patient to hospital
B. Administer tetracycline, refer the patient to hospital
C. Administer intravenous fluid, administer antibiotic
D. Take stool for culture, administer tetracycline
Answer: A. Administer intravenous fluid, refer the patient to hospital
Rationale: The immediate life-saving priority in confirmed cholera at a health centre is aggressive rehydration with IV fluids (Ringer's lactate) to correct hypovolaemia, followed by referral for continued management (Kenya MOH cholera guidelines). Antibiotics (doxycycline) are an adjunct that shortens illness and reduces shedding but are not the immediate life-saving intervention. Therefore administer IV fluid and refer is the correct immediate care.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
232. Signs and symptoms of rabies include;
A. History of a dog bite, dribbling of saliva, difficulty in swallowing
B. Itching at the site of the wound, fever, irritation and skin rash
C. Difficulty in swallowing , history of dog bite, slow pulse
D. Severe pain at the site of the wound, difficulty in swallowing, dribbling of saliva
Answer: A. History of a dog bite, dribbling of saliva, difficulty in swallowing
Rationale: Rabies classically presents with a history of animal (dog) bite, hydrophobia with difficulty swallowing, and excessive salivation/drooling due to pharyngeal spasm. Option A captures the pathognomonic triad. Per Kenya MOH communicable disease guidelines these are the hallmark features of clinical rabies.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
233. The fat soluble vitamins are;
A. Vitamins A,B, C and D
B. Vitamins, D,K,B and C
C. Vitamins B,A,C and K
D. Vitamins E,D,A and K
Answer: D. Vitamins E,D,A and K
Rationale: The fat-soluble vitamins are A, D, E and K (mnemonic ADEK). Only option D lists exclusively fat-soluble vitamins; the others incorrectly include water-soluble vitamins B and C. Per Kenya Nutrition guidelines and standard nutrition texts.
234. The following statement is false about hard immunity:-
A. Results from low infection pressure.
B. It is applicable in all immunizable diseases.
C. Occurs when the percentage of immunized people is high
D. It is achieved by keeping the susceptible cases low
Answer: B. It is applicable in all immunizable diseases.
Rationale: Herd (herd/'hard') immunity does NOT apply to all immunizable diseases; it only works for diseases spread person-to-person, not for those with non-human reservoirs (e.g., tetanus, where herd immunity does not protect). It results from high immunization coverage, low infection pressure and keeping susceptibles low. Thus the false statement is B. Per epidemiology/PHC principles.
235. The elements of PHC are:-
A. Maternal child health, nutrition, appropriate technology
B. Immunization, education, community rehabilitation
C. Malaria control, essential drugs supply, equitable distribution of resources
D. Mental health, integration of services, local disease control
Answer: A. Maternal child health, nutrition, appropriate technology
Rationale: The Alma-Ata elements of PHC include MCH/family planning, nutrition, health education, safe water/sanitation, immunization, control of endemic diseases, essential drugs, and treatment of common diseases. Only option A lists genuine PHC elements (MCH, nutrition, appropriate technology). Per Kenya PHC/community health nursing curriculum.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
236. Close contacts for a patient with Tuberculosis should always  be screened, this is:-
A. Primary prevention
B. Tertiary prevention
C. Secondary prevention
D. Intermediate prevention
Answer: C. Secondary prevention
Rationale: Screening close contacts of a TB patient aims for early detection of disease in asymptomatic/exposed individuals, which is secondary prevention. Primary prevention prevents onset (e.g., BCG), tertiary limits complications. Per Kenya TB programme and levels-of-prevention framework.
237. Which of the following is the correct order of community mobilization steps
A. Planning, community entry, conducting, monitoring
B. Community entry, planning, conducting, monitoring
C. Making reinforcement, community entry, planning, conducting
D. Community entry, planning, monitoring, conducting
Answer: B. Community entry, planning, conducting, monitoring
Rationale: Community mobilization begins with community entry (establishing rapport/entry point), followed by planning, conducting/implementing activities, then monitoring and evaluation. Option B gives this correct logical sequence. Per Kenya community health nursing/community strategy guidelines.
238. The correct sequence of decision making steps is;
A. Gather information, define problem, develop alternatives, decide
B. Define problem, gather information, develop alternatives, decide
C. Decide, gather information, define problem, develop alternatives
D. Develop alternatives, decide, gather information, define the problem
Answer: B. Define problem, gather information, develop alternatives, decide
Rationale: Rational decision-making starts by defining/identifying the problem, then gathering relevant information, developing/weighing alternatives, and finally deciding (and implementing/evaluating). Option B follows this correct order. Per Nursing Management and Leadership principles.
239. Which is the best time to terminate a home visit?
A. When another care provider gives care to your client.
B. When the income level of your client goes up.
C. When the client becomes self sufficient in maintaining their health.
D. When other the members of the family develop same condition as client.
Answer: C. When the client becomes self sufficient in maintaining their health.
Rationale: A home visit/nurse-client relationship is terminated when the client has achieved the set goals and is self-sufficient in maintaining their own health, reflecting the empowerment/independence aim of community health nursing. Per Kenya community health nursing principles.
240. Which among the following statements, best describes how resources should be distributed according to PHC principles.
A. Equally among all community members with needs.
B. As perceived by the community leaders regardless of community felt needs.
C. Fairly among all individuals and families to help them meet their fundamental basic needs.
D. As perceived by donor community.
Answer: C. Fairly among all individuals and families to help them meet their fundamental basic needs.
Rationale: A core PHC principle is equity — distributing resources fairly according to need so that all individuals and families can meet fundamental basic needs, not strictly equally. This reflects the Alma-Ata declaration and Kenya PHC/community health strategy.
241. The following are important in ensuring active community participation;
A. Giving roles to new members as they come to join in community projects
B. Ignore new comers ideas that had already been discussed before to avoid repetition
C. and save on time
Answer: A. Giving roles to new members as they come to join in community projects
Rationale: Active community participation is fostered by inclusiveness — assigning roles to new members and valuing their ideas, rather than ignoring newcomers. Ignoring new members' contributions undermines empowerment, a key community health strategy principle.
242. When formulating FGD guides for data collection, one should;
A. Use close ended questions allowing simple and straight answers.
B. Allow elite members of the community to dominate the discussion to ensure accurate information is collected.
Answer: A. Use close ended questions allowing simple and straight answers.
Rationale: Correct FGD facilitation uses open-ended, probing questions to elicit rich discussion and actively prevents any subgroup (including elite members) from dominating so all voices are heard (research methods for community health). Both provided options describe poor practice: closed questions restrict discussion and allowing elite dominance biases the data. The correct guidance is therefore not among the options.
⚠ Correct answer NOT among the provided choices  ⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
243. The uppermost shelf of the main compartment of the KEPI refrigerator is suitable for the storage of:
A. Oral polio and BCG vaccines
B. Oral polio and Measles vaccines
C. Oral polio and DPT vaccines
D. Oral polio and Tetanus toxoid vaccines
Answer: B. Oral polio and Measles vaccines
Rationale: In the KEPI cold-chain refrigerator the uppermost/top shelf of the main compartment is coldest and is used for the most heat-sensitive, freeze-safe vaccines, namely OPV and Measles (Kenya KEPI cold chain guidelines). DPT, TT (DT/Td) and other diluent-containing or freeze-sensitive vaccines are stored on lower shelves away from the freezing zone, and BCG is not paired with OPV on the top shelf in the standard arrangement. Options are present in this version, so the item is answerable: OPV and Measles is correct.
244. Nutrition is defined as:
A. Any substance that may be eaten
B. b1. A balanced diet of adequate amount
C. Body’s food requirement
D. Science of food and its effects on the body
Answer: D. Science of food and its effects on the body
Rationale: Nutrition is defined as the science of food and its relationship to/effects on health and the body, including ingestion, digestion, absorption and utilization of nutrients. This is the standard definition per Kenya nutrition and Kozier fundamentals texts.
245. The following laboratory test involves antigen-antibody reaction:
A. Alcohol Fast Bacilli Test.
B. Widal test
C. Gram Staining.
D. International Normalizing ratio (INR)
Answer: B. Widal test
Rationale: The Widal test detects antibodies against Salmonella typhi O and H antigens through an antigen-antibody agglutination reaction. AFB and Gram staining are microscopy/staining techniques, and INR is a coagulation (clotting) measurement. Reference: Kenya Pharmacology/Microbiology guidelines.
246. Skills needed in critical thinking include
A. Preference
B. Inference
C. Self indulgence
D. Adherence
Answer: B. Inference
Rationale: Inference (drawing logical conclusions from evidence) is a core cognitive skill of critical thinking, along with interpretation, analysis and evaluation (Kozier Fundamentals). Preference, self-indulgence and adherence are not critical-thinking skills.
247. Components of the communication process include;
A. Mediator, source, receiver
B. Feedback, social setting, amplifier
C. Channel, message, source
D. Speaker, audience, recorder
Answer: C. Channel, message, source
Rationale: The core components of the communication process are the source (sender), the message, the channel, the receiver and feedback. Only option C lists genuine components (channel, message, source); the others include non-standard terms like amplifier and recorder (Kozier Fundamentals).
248. When writing a report the following should be considered except:
A. Language to be used in the report
B. Type of information to be included
C. Time the report shall be presented
D. Purpose of writing the report
Answer: C. Time the report shall be presented
Rationale: When writing a report the writer must consider language, type/content of information and purpose. The time the report will be presented is a scheduling/logistical matter, not a writing consideration, so it is the exception.
249. Barriers to effective communication originating from the source include;
A. Restlessness among the audience
B. Message too long
C. Inadequate knowledge on the subject area
D. Negative attitude towards the message
Answer: C. Inadequate knowledge on the subject area
Rationale: The source (sender) of communication introduces barriers such as inadequate knowledge of the subject, which impairs message encoding. Restlessness and negative attitude are receiver/audience barriers, while an overly long message is a barrier of the message/channel. Reference: Kenya community health promotion and NCK communication principles.
250. The micro-organisms that are minimised through hand-washing include;
A. Streptococcus pyogenes.
B. Staphylococcus albus
C. Escherichia coli.
D. Entamoeba histolytica
Answer: A. Streptococcus pyogenes.
Rationale: Hand-washing removes transient flora acquired on the skin, chiefly pathogens such as Streptococcus pyogenes, and reduces cross-transmission. Staphylococcus albus (epidermidis) is resident (permanent) flora less affected by routine washing, while E. coli and Entamoeba histolytica are faecal organisms typically addressed but the classic transient pathogen minimised is S. pyogenes. Per Kenya IPC guidelines and Kozier Fundamentals.
⚠ Ambiguous — more than one defensible answer
